# Canine babesiosis dataset catalogue

[Back to article index](index.md) · [Research map](research-map.md) · [datasets.jsonl](datasets.jsonl) · [download manifest](data/manifest.jsonl)

Verification date: 2026-10-23 (rounds 1–3) and 2026-10-28 (round 4, articles published 2006–2016). Files were downloaded only where public and ≤50 MB (total cap 250 MB); original bytes and SHA-256 checksums are in [data/manifest.jsonl](data/manifest.jsonl) and structure summaries in `data/inspection/`. No authors were contacted, no access controls were bypassed and no terms were accepted. Large raw omics files (PRIDE RAW, SRA FASTQ) were **not** downloaded; their retrieval route is given below.

## Summary

- **21 verified-download dataset records with individual dog/sample-level data**, representing **15 independent animal sources** (records sharing a source letter overlap in animals; see `independent_source`).
- 14 further verified downloads contain only summary statistics, protein lists or no data (listed separately; not patient-level).
- 12 public records verified without file retrieval (mostly parasite-side genomic/proteomic resources without host phenotypes).
- 10 articles state data are available on request (not pursued).
- Remaining included articles: 210 with no dataset located after documented checks (status 4) and 191 not assessable because full text was inaccessible (status 5).

No dataset combines a large independent cohort, a hard outcome, rich multi-system phenotypes and omics. Every verified dataset is small relative to its feature count; most are suited to effect-size estimation, mixed models and hypothesis generation rather than predictive ML. Exploratory clusters from any of them must not be described as disease subtypes.

Ranking weighs mechanistic relevance first, then independent sample size, phenotype richness (outcomes, multi-system variables, time points), documentation, reuse terms and analytical feasibility.

## Dataset entries

<a id="s-cb050"></a>
### S-CB050 — Supplementary Table 1 (Table_1.DOCX) of Celliers et al. 2020, Neutrophil myeloperoxidase index in dogs with Babesia rossi babesiosis (Frontiers Vet Sci 7:72), Europe PMC supplementary zip PMC7040022

**Why ranked 1:** Largest verified B. rossi cohort with a hard outcome (survival) and admission cytokines/MPXI; one row per dog.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Public download verified |
| Linked articles | [CB050](articles/CB050_2020_neutrophil-myeloperoxidase-index-babesiosis-caused-babesia.md) |
| Correspondence check | Corresponds, with a one-dog discrepancy. The Word table has 161 data rows with 161 unique Case IDs: 141 Infected (126 Survived, 15 Died), 20 Control. The article note gives 140 infected dogs and 20 controls, 15/140 deaths (11%), cohort 1 = 96 dogs + 15 controls and cohort 2 = 44 dogs + 5 controls. In the file, cases 1-97 (97 infected) and 101-144 (44 infected) correspond to cohorts 1 and 2; controls 201-215 (15, with cytokines) and 216-220 (5, no cytokines) match the 15 + 5 control split. Cohort 2 has 44 dogs with no cytokine data, which fits the note. The cohort-1 infected block has 97 rows against 96 reported, so the file holds 141 infected dogs against 140 reported. I could not tell whether this is an article counting discrepancy or a row included in the supplement but not the analysis; case numbers jump 97 to 101, so some IDs are absent. Variables match the article: MPXI, segmented and band neutrophils, and seven cytokines/chemokines (IL-2, IL-6, IL-8, IL-10, IL-18, GM-CSF, MCP-1). Mortality 15/141 = 10.6%, consistent with the reported 11%. Single time point per dog, as in the article. |
| Repository / accession | Europe PMC supplementary files for PMC7040022 (Frontiers in Veterinary Science supplement) / DOI 10.3389/fvets.2020.00072; PMCID PMC7040022; PMID 32133380 |
| Landing page | https://europepmc.org/article/PMC/PMC7040022 |
| Licence / reuse terms | CC BY (Europe PMC record for PMC7040022 returns license = "cc by", isOpenAccess = Y; version not stated). This is the article licence and is assumed to cover the supplement; no separate dataset licence is stated. Read from the Europe PMC REST search core result on 2026-10-23. |
| Species | Domestic dog (Canis lupus familiaris), client-owned, naturally infected with Babesia rossi (PCR and reverse line blot per the article note); South Africa (Onderstepoort) |
| Design | Retrospective pooling of two prior hospital cohorts (Oct 2011 to Apr 2013, and Jan to Dec 2014) of naturally infected dogs plus healthy controls. Cross-sectional: one admission measurement per dog (haematology with ADVIA MPXI, plus a 7-analyte multiplex cytokine/chemokine panel in a subset). Outcome is binary survival, which includes euthanasia for poor prognosis per the article note. |
| Granularity | individual dog/sample-level |
| Raw vs processed | Individual-level observed values, presented as a published supplementary table. This is not a raw instrument export. Cytokine values are censored or imputed: repeated floor values (IL-2 3.4, IL-6 3.6, GM-CSF 9.1, IL-18 5.7, IL-10 8.4 pg/mL) appear to be assay detection limits or substituted values, and 4 cells hold the ceiling value 50001.0 (case 80 for IL-2, IL-6, IL-18 and GM-CSF). Decimal commas are used. The zip also holds figure images (2 GIF, 2 JPG) that carry no data. |
| Independent animals | 161 — Unique Case IDs in the single DOCX table, counted programmatically from the read-only dump: 161 rows with 161 unique IDs = 141 infected + 20 controls. One row per dog and no repeated measures. The count of 141 infected differs from the article's 140. Cytokine data exist for 112 dogs: 97 infected and 15 controls. |
| Samples | 161 |
| Groups and controls | Infected survivors n=126; infected non-survivors n=15 (12 of them have cytokine data); healthy controls n=20 (15 with cytokines, 5 without). Sex: infected 93 M / 48 F; controls 7 M / 13 F. Controls are healthy dogs, but selection criteria and how they were matched on age, weight or breed are not given in the table. Other columns: Age (months), Weight (kg), Gender, Status (Infected/Control), Outcome (Survived/Died/Control). |
| Time points | Single time point per dog (presumably admission, before treatment, but this is not stated in the file). No longitudinal data. |
| Features | 17 columns: Case ID; Age (mos); Weight (kg); Gender; Status; Outcome; WBC, segmented neutrophils and band neutrophils (x10^9/L); IL-2, IL-6, IL-8, IL-10, IL-18, GM-CSF, MCP-1 (pg/mL); MPXI (unitless ADVIA index). That is 6 demographic/label columns, 3 haematology counts, 7 cytokines/chemokines and 1 MPXI. Breed, parasitaemia, clinical severity score, other haematology (haematocrit, platelets), biochemistry, and treatment are absent. |
| Outcomes | Binary survival (Survived vs Died; 15/141 deaths in infected dogs) and the infected vs control label. There is no complicated vs uncomplicated classification or severity score in the file. |
| Data dictionary | Partial. Column headers carry units (months, kg, x10^9/L, pg/mL), and Status and Outcome use plain-text codes. There is no separate dictionary and no definition of how below-detection and above-range values were handled, no MPXI reference or definition, and no analyser or assay details, which are in the article only. Blank cells are not explained. |
| Missingness | Cytokines (7 columns) are blank in 49/161 dogs: 44 infected (cohort 2) and 5 controls (216-220), so they are missing by cohort design and not at random. Weight is blank in 10 dogs (9 infected, 1 control). Age is blank in 1 infected dog (case 56). MPXI is blank in 1 infected dog (case 77). WBC, neutrophil counts, sex, status and outcome are complete. Case IDs skip 98-100 and 145-200, so some dogs were apparently excluded. Cytokine values at the floor are frequent: IL-2 at 3.4 in about 30 dogs and GM-CSF at 9.1 in about 28. These appear to be left-censored or imputed values, which distorts distributions and correlations. |
| Technical requirements | Minimal. The DOCX table has to be parsed (python-docx or a similar tool; all values are strings). Convert decimal commas to points, treat 50001.0 as right-censored and the repeated floor values as left-censored, and cohort identity has to be inferred from case numbers 1-97 / 101-144 / 201-215 / 216-220. Fits in memory and needs no omics annotation. Censoring-aware or rank-based methods are needed. |
| Files | PMC7040022_supplementaryFiles.zip, 247,154 bytes, SHA-256 8113a933faceb155103230eaea433f6c5b34ad7aba71531282c01707df63eb85. It contains Table_1.DOCX (218,258 bytes; one table of 162 rows including the header and 17 columns) and four figure files, fvets-07-00072-g0001 and g0002 as GIF and JPG (6.7 to 39 kB each), which are article figures with no tabular data. |
| Overlaps | The dogs come from two previously published cohorts: Goddard et al. 2016 PLoS ONE (96 dogs + 15 controls) and Rautenbach et al. 2017 Vet Parasitol (44 dogs + 5 controls). The same dogs and cytokine values very likely appear in those papers' data, so the datasets are not independent and must not be pooled as separate cohorts. Within this file there are no repeated dogs. The cohort-1 cytokine panel comes from Goddard et al. |

**Downloaded files (1; 0.25 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB050/PMC7040022_supplementaryFiles.zip` (247,154 B, sha256 `8113a933face…`)

**Suitable questions:** (1) Whether MPXI, neutrophil counts and the seven cytokines differ between infected dogs and controls, and between survivors, non-survivors and controls. (2) Univariable and adjusted associations of admission cytokines and MPXI with mortality in B. rossi babesiosis. (3) The correlation structure between MPXI and cytokines, by outcome. (4) Whether there is a cytokine-MPXI signature of non-survivors that could feed an exploratory hypothesis. Use it as a small, individual-level reference table of innate-immune/neutrophil activation markers for B. rossi in South African dogs.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are more defensible than ML here. Use Mann-Whitney or Kruskal-Wallis with exact or permutation tests and multiplicity control (Holm/BH), and effect sizes with bootstrap CIs. For prognosis, use log-transformed predictors in firth or penalised logistic regression (ridge/lasso) adjusted for age and weight, with only about 15 events (about 12 with cytokines) so very few predictors per model; report validation by repeated nested CV or bootstrap optimism correction, and AUC with CIs. Treat floor and ceiling values as censored (Tobit or rank methods), and handle cohort 2 as structurally missing for cytokines (complete-case or cohort-stratified analysis; do not impute). Include cohort as a covariate or stratum. Spearman correlations need a multiplicity adjustment, and within-group correlations in 12 to 15 dogs are unstable. Unsupervised clustering is not advised; any clusters found would be exploratory and not subtypes.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n relative to the 7+ correlated predictors: 15 deaths overall and 12 with cytokine data, so ML or multivariable prediction would overfit, and a held-out test set is not feasible; use animal-level resampling (one row per dog, so animal-level splitting is automatically satisfied, but there is no external validation set). Two pooled cohorts from different years (batch and assay-run effects are confounded with cohort and cannot be separated; cytokine data only in cohort 1 infected dogs, and 5 controls lack them). Controls are older/heavier on average than infected dogs? This is not verified; control selection and matching are undocumented, so group comparisons may be confounded by age, weight and sex. Selection bias: tertiary referral hospital, and euthanasia for poor prognosis in the Died label means the outcome partly reflects owner or clinician decisions rather than biology. Treatment effects: the sampling time relative to treatment is not stated in the file; if post-treatment, cytokines and MPXI could be treatment-altered. Outcome leakage: the file has no post-outcome variables, but the neutrophil counts are components of WBC and MPXI is derived from the same analyser run, so they are collinear. Censoring/imputation of cytokines (floor values, 50001 ceiling in case 80) is undocumented in the file. 1-dog count discrepancy against the article (141 vs 140). Cross-species comparability: the ADVIA MPXI uses a human-derived reference algorithm and the canine multiplex cytokine assay is not directly comparable with human assays; MPXI is a cell-population average and not a mechanism; breed, parasitaemia and co-morbidity are absent. Also, the Word table has no machine-readable structure beyond strings. The data are only observational and associational.

**Unverified fields:** Whether the extra infected dog (141 vs 140 in the article) is an article/supplement mismatch or an excluded dog; Timing of sampling relative to treatment and relative to onset of illness; Exact meaning of the floor values (3.4, 3.6, 8.4, 9.1, 5.7) and of 50001.0 (detection limit vs imputation); Control-dog selection and matching criteria; Whether the supplement licence differs from the article CC BY licence (CC BY version not stated in Europe PMC record); Cohort assignment by case-ID range (inferred from counts, not stated in the file); Content of the figure images (not opened; assumed to be article figures); Whether the same dogs appear in the Goddard 2016 and Rautenbach 2017 datasets (inferred from the article note, not checked against those papers)

<a id="ds01"></a>
### DS01 — Experimental Babesia rossi infection data (Mendeley Data; raw per-dog time-series workbook plus supplementary group-summary table)

**Why ranked 2:** Controlled experimental B. rossi infection: daily multi-system clinical, haematology, lactate, cell-free Hb and 13-plex cytokine time series.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Public download verified |
| Linked articles | [CB101](articles/CB101_2022_dataset-inflammatory-response-during-experimental-infection.md), [CB098](articles/CB098_2022_kinetics-inflammatory-response-during-experimental-babesia.md) |
| Correspondence check | Verified against CB101 full text (Europe PMC XML, PMC9508437), which names this Mendeley DOI as its raw-data source and the docx as its 'Supplementary data' table. CB098 is the companion analytic paper (full text not accessed; abstract only), so CB098 correspondence rests on CB101's statement that it describes the same study. (1) Design matches. The xlsx key lists 6 dogs: 1 splenectomised donor (Banjo), 2 low-dose (Chuckels, Einstein), 3 high-dose (Frank, Hamlet, Ozzy). CB101 and CB098 give 1 donor, 2 LD and 3 HD male Beagles. (2) Group means and ranges in the docx were recomputed from xlsx rows and match. Dogs 2-3 are LD and dogs 4-6 are HD, assuming rows follow dog-number order (see unverified_fields).  - Temperature, HD at 24 h: 38.1, 37.1 and 38.0 give 37.73, as in docx.  - Temperature, HD at 96 h: 40.7, 39.7 and 36.4 give 38.93 (36.4-40.7), as in docx.  - Haematocrit, HD at 96 h: 0.28, 0.27 and 0.12 give 0.22 (0.12-0.28).  - Parasitaemia, HD at 48 h, 72 h and 96 h: 0.916, 10.877 and 46.763, all matching.  - Mature neutrophils, HD at 144 h and 168 h: 7.23 and 17.64, matching.  - Docx 'Baseline' equals the per-dog mean of columns -1 and 0, and docx hour labels map to xlsx day columns (24 h = day 1, 96 h = day 4, 108 h = day 4.1, 192 h = day 8). (3) The dog that died is visible in the data. Dog 6, the third HD dog, has values only through day 4 (Hct 0.12, lactate 7.5, temperature 36.4), matching the article's 'one HD dog died on day 4'. HD dogs have no 108 h value (docx 'NA'), and the LD dogs have day 4.1 values, matching 'HD treated at 96 h, LD at 108 h'. (4) The 13 cytokines in the xlsx (GM-CSF, IFN-g, IL-2, 6, 7, 8, 10, 15, 18, IP-10, KC-like, MCP-1, TNF-a) match the CB101 list exactly. Discrepancies: (a) The xlsx labels the HD dose '10^9', while CB101 text states 10^8 parasitised RBC (1.14 mL), so the label is probably a typo. (b) CRP, albumin, total protein, blood-gas variables other than lactate, and the day 5-8 parasitaemia values are in the docx or article but not in the xlsx. (c) CB101 says the splenectomised donor was excluded from data collection, yet each clinical and CBC block has one lone single-time-point row that is probably the donor. |
| Repository / accession | Mendeley Data / 10.17632/zyhy8z69kt.1 |
| Landing page | https://doi.org/10.17632/zyhy8z69kt.1 |
| Licence / reuse terms | CC BY 4.0 (from the Mendeley API data_licence metadata supplied with the task; the Mendeley record page was not re-read). The linked data article CB101 (PMC9508437) is also licensed cc by, per the Europe PMC record license field (isOpenAccess = Y). CB098 (Vet Parasitol) is not in PMC and its licence was not checked. |
| Species | Dog (Canis lupus familiaris): purpose-bred intact male Beagles, University of Pretoria Biomedical Research Centre (Onderstepoort, South Africa). Pathogen: Babesia rossi (cryopreserved wild-type isolate, PCR-RLB mono-infection per CB101). |
| Design | Prospective longitudinal experimental infection, not natural disease. - A splenectomised donor raised the inoculum. - Five spleen-intact Beagles were randomised to low dose (10^4 parasitised RBC IV, n=2) or high dose (10^8 per article text, n=3). - Sampling was daily from day 0 for CBC and clinical variables. Serum biochemistry and venous blood gas were every second day, and plasma was stored at -80 C for a batched 13-plex canine Milliplex/Luminex cytokine assay. - Every dog was treated at a preset endpoint: diminazene aceturate 3.5 mg/kg SC once, plus supportive care and transfusion as needed. HD dogs were treated at 96 h and LD dogs at 108 h, the latter despite less severe disease. One HD dog (dog 6) died on day 4. - Baseline is two pre-infection samplings per dog. Dogs act as their own controls. - Dose groups are unbalanced (2 vs 3) and treatment time is fixed by the day-4 events, so dose, disease stage and treatment are confounded after day 4. |
| Granularity | individual dog/sample-level, longitudinal, aggregate study-level, summary statistics only |
| Raw vs processed | Raw data.xlsx holds individual-dog, per-time-point observed values (raw/near-raw: clinical scores, vitals, CBC, glucose, lactate, cell-free Hb, parasitaemia %, cytokine concentrations after the authors' detection-limit substitution). Supplementary data.docx is processed, group-level only: mean (range) per dose group with mixed-model p values for LD vs HD, LD vs baseline and HD vs baseline (2 tables, about 25-33 rows each, 10 time columns), and it includes CRP, which is absent from the xlsx. No raw Luminex MFI or standard-curve files are provided. The docx p values come from n=2 vs n=3 dogs and should not be reused as evidence. |
| Independent animals | 6 — The xlsx key table lists 6 dogs (numbers 1-6). Value blocks carry no dog-ID column; row order (dog 2 to 6) is inferred and cross-validated against docx group statistics. Five infected dogs have longitudinal data: 2 LD and 3 HD, of which dog 6 stops at day 4 (died). One extra lone row per clinical and CBC block (a single time point per variable) is presumed to be the splenectomised donor (dog 1) but is not labelled. Dogs are the independent unit: n=5 for any infection analysis, n=2 per LD arm and n=3 per HD arm. All repeated rows belong to these dogs. |
| Samples | 48 |
| Groups and controls | LD (10^4 pRBC): dogs 2 and 3. HD (10^8 per article, labelled 10^9 in xlsx): dogs 4, 5 and 6. There is no uninfected control group and no sham, and no untreated infected arm because all dogs were treated at the endpoint. The pre-infection baseline (day -1 and day 0 columns, collected as the within-dog reference) is the only control. The splenectomised donor (dog 1) is not an experimental arm. Dog 6 (HD) died on day 4, so it has no post-treatment data. |
| Time points | The xlsx has 11 columns: day -1, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 4.1 (108 h, LD only), 5, 6, 7, 8. The docx names 10 columns: baseline (mean of day -1 and day 0), 24, 48, 72, 96, 108, 120, 144, 168 and 192 h. Treatment was at 96 h (HD) and 108 h (LD), so day 5-8 data are post-treatment. - Clinical, haematology, BP, glucose: day -1 to day 8, with 4.1 for the 2 LD dogs only. - Cytokines: days -1, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 only (8 time points; no 4.1, 5 or 7). - Lactate: day 1-6 and 8 (no baseline). Cell-free Hb: day 1-6 and 8, sparse. - Parasitaemia in xlsx: day 1-4.1 plus one later 0 value, so day 5-8 parasitaemia is docx-only. - Dog 6 stops at day 4, and the dog 1 row has day -1 only. |
| Features | About 35 variable blocks in one stacked sheet (rows are dogs, columns are time points; no ID column). - Clinical examination (12): habitus (1-4), appetite (1-4), temperature (C), heart rate, respiratory rate, mucous membrane (1-4 key), capillary refill time, femoral pulse (1-3 key), faecal consistency (1-3 key), SBP, DBP and MAP. - Haematology and biochemistry (9): glucose (mmol/L; point-of-care), venous lactate, haematocrit (L/L), WCC, mature neutrophils, band neutrophils, lymphocytes, monocytes, parasitaemia (%). - Cell-free Hb: 1 block, units not stated. - Cytokines/chemokines (13 blocks, units not stated; presumably pg/mL from the Milliplex kit): GM-CSF, IFN-g, KC-like, IP-10, IL-2, IL-6, IL-7, IL-8, IL-10, IL-15, IL-18, MCP-1, TNF-a. - In the docx only: CRP (mg/L). - In CB101 only (not in xlsx): albumin, total protein, other blood-gas variables. Data points: clinical blocks have 49 values each, 13 cytokine blocks have 38 values each (494 in total), lactate 30, cell-free Hb 23 and parasitaemia 27. |
| Outcomes | No explicit outcome or severity column. - Survival is derivable (dog 6 died on day 4; the others survived), but it is a single event in n=5. - Severity proxies: habitus score, appetite score, Hct nadir, parasitaemia peak, lactate (7.5 in dog 6 at day 4), MAP and so on. - Humane endpoints defined in the article (Hct 10-15%, habitus 1+, neurological signs, ARDS, creatinine > 200, haemoconcentration) triggered treatment. - There is no complicated vs uncomplicated label and no organ-dysfunction labels. Dose group is the only categorical label in the file. |
| Data dictionary | Partial and weak inside the xlsx. - Present: a dog name/number/procedure key, and category keys for mucous membrane (1 pale, 2 icteric, 3 normal, 4 congested), femoral pulse (1 absent, 2 weak, 3 strong) and faecal consistency (1 diarrhoea, 2 normal, 3 firm). The 'Exp group 0=low dose; 1=' key is truncated and the 'infection date' and 'experiment start date' cells are empty. - Absent from the xlsx: units, the habitus and appetite scale definitions, row-to-dog mapping and the cytokine detection-limit rule. The time-column codes (H-1, H0 ... H4.1) are not explained. - Present elsewhere: the docx gives units per variable (including CRP) and the habitus and appetite scale ranges; CB101 gives scale definitions, the assay, the detection-limit rule and the sampling schedule. |
| Missingness | Moderate and mostly structural. - Clinical, CBC and BP blocks: 49 of 66 possible cells filled (about 26% empty), because dogs 4 and 5 have no day 4.1 value and dog 6 has none after day 4. - Cytokine blocks: 38 of 55 filled (31% empty); missing days are by design. - Late-sparse blocks: lactate (30 values), cell-free Hb (23) and parasitaemia (27) are sparse. - Glucose is missing at day 4 for the 2 LD dogs (day 4.1 value present instead). - Dog 6 missingness is informative, since death truncated its series. Quality flags: - Monocyte count for dog 2 on day 7 is 68, while the docx range for LD at 168 h is 0.53-0.68 (0.68 appears to be a data-entry error). - Day 0 WCC and lymphocytes are elevated in all five dogs (WCC 9.7-25.0, lymphocytes 6.3-15.0), likely excitement or handling leukocytosis at the inoculation visit, which inflates the baseline. - Dog 5 cytokines at days 6 and 8 show implausible extremes (IL-2 143,098 and 354,790; IL-6 35,960 and 60,728; MCP-1 98,603 and 227,246; GM-CSF 9,220.8 repeated) beside IL-15 and IL-18 values of 0, so assay saturation, extrapolation or dilution artefacts are likely. - Values at or below the detection limit were set to the minimum detectable concentration or to 0 (per CB101 text), so zeros are not true zeros. |
| Technical requirements | Low. Plain spreadsheet and Word files that open in pandas/openpyxl and python-docx; no CanFam annotation, proteomics or sequence resources. The xlsx is one sheet of stacked blocks (header row, then 5-6 unlabelled dog rows per variable) and needs custom reshaping to long format (dog x time x variable). The dog mapping must be assigned by row order and checked against the docx. No embedded macros were inspected; files were read only through the read-only table helper. Sizes: xlsx 23,564 B, docx 36,913 B. |
| Files | 1) Raw data.xlsx, 23,564 B (SHA-256 bde6f704...e52f), one sheet 'Sheet1', 284 rows x 11 columns. Dog key, category key, then about 35 variable blocks in wide format. 2) Supplementary data.docx, 36,913 B (SHA-256 3a37310a...5595), 2 tables (33 and 25 rows) of group mean (range) per time point plus mixed-model p values; 10 time columns; includes CRP. Both were retrieved 2026-10-23 and read. |
| Overlaps | Same experiment as CB098 (the companion analytic paper, which uses the same dogs and cytokines) and CB101 (the data article). The two publications describe one dataset, not independent data. All time-point rows are repeated measures of the same 5 dogs, plus one presumed donor row. The docx summaries are derived from the xlsx and should not be double-counted. It could be compared with other experimental canine Babesia models, but no dog-level overlap with other candidate datasets is known. |

**Downloaded files (2; 0.06 MB):** `data/raw/DS01/Raw data.xlsx` (23,564 B, sha256 `bde6f70431a4…`); `data/raw/DS01/Supplementary data.docx` (36,913 B, sha256 `3a37310a3e7c…`)

**Suitable questions:** Hypothesis-generating, within-dog questions in a controlled B. rossi time course: - Temporal ordering of cytokine, chemokine, CRP, haematology and haemodynamic changes relative to parasitaemia and clinical decline. - Descriptive dose-response of timing and magnitude (LD vs HD). - Pre- vs post-treatment cytokine kinetics (treatment-associated rise; day 6 and 8 cytokines). - Relationships of lactate, MAP, haematocrit and cell-free Hb with parasitaemia and habitus score. - Dog-level trajectory plots. - Supplying effect-size priors and variance estimates for sample-size calculation in a future B. rossi study. Not suitable for biomarker discovery or prognostic modelling, or for natural-infection severity subtypes.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are far more defensible than ML. - Preferred: dog-level descriptive trajectory plots, linear mixed models with a random intercept per dog (as the authors did), or nonparametric repeated-measures approaches (e.g. Friedman or rank-based within-dog contrasts) with exact or permutation tests, and effect sizes with CIs rather than p values. - Cytokines should be log- or rank-transformed after handling detection-limit substitutions and the dog 5 outliers. Sensitivity analyses should exclude dog 6 and the day 6 and 8 outliers. - Do not run unsupervised clustering to find 'subtypes'. Any grouping of five dogs would be exploratory, would be driven by dose group, and cannot support subtype claims. - If ML is explored at all, it should be limited to penalised regression with leave-one-dog-out nested CV, interpreted only descriptively.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** - Small n, high p: 5 infected dogs (2 vs 3) against roughly 35 variables and 13 cytokines. The authors' p values (e.g. <0.001 from 2 vs 3 dogs) are pseudo-replicated and fragile. - Repeated measures: about 48 rows come from 5 dogs, so splitting must be at animal level (leave-one-dog-out); row-wise CV leaks. - Treatment confounding: all dogs were treated on day 4 at humane or experimental endpoints (HD at 96 h; LD at 108 h, earlier in disease), so dose, disease stage and treatment effect are confounded. Day 5-8 samples are post-treatment and must not be used to predict pre-treatment severity. - Outcome leakage: haematocrit and habitus are humane-endpoint criteria that define the treatment time and fate, so using them to predict severity is circular. Dog 6's death means missing post-day-4 data are informative. - No untreated infected or uninfected control; baseline is two pre-infection samples with a day 0 leukocytosis artefact. - Batch effects: cytokines were batched on stored plasma in a single Luminex run, so assay-plate effects cannot be separated from time. Detection-limit imputation and saturated, extrapolated values (dog 5 days 6 and 8) distort distributions. - Documentation gaps: no row IDs, no units in the xlsx, an HD dose label of 10^9 vs 10^8 in the text, and CRP and albumin absent from the raw file. - Generalisability: male Beagles, one B. rossi isolate, a single donor, intravenous inoculation, and a short (about 8 day) course. Cross-species comparability with natural canine disease, human malaria or zoonotic babesiosis is uncertain, and cytokine units and platform are not harmonised with other datasets.

**Unverified fields:** Row-to-dog mapping in the xlsx (rows are unlabelled; inferred as dogs 2-6 in order, supported by docx group statistics and dog 6 truncation, but dog 1 identity of lone rows is presumed); HD dose (xlsx label 10^9 versus article text 10^8; the correct value is from the article); Units for cytokines (pg/mL presumed) and cell-free haemoglobin (not stated in file); Time-column code meaning (H-1, H0 ... H4.1 mapped to days via docx comparison; docx 'baseline' = mean of day -1 and day 0 inferred from recomputation); Licence read only from supplied Mendeley API metadata, not re-checked on the Mendeley page; CB098 full text not accessed (correspondence to CB098 rests on CB101 and abstract); CB098 licence unknown; Parasitaemia day 5-8 values appear in docx but not in xlsx; whether a trailing 0 in xlsx is day 6 or day 7 is unverified; Whether the xlsx lone first-row values belong to the splenectomised donor; Cause of dog 5 extreme cytokine values (days 6 and 8) and the dog 2 monocyte value of 68; Hidden sheets, comments or cell formatting in the xlsx beyond what the table helper shows; Exact treatment/supportive therapy per dog (transfusions given to which dogs)

<a id="ds06"></a>
### DS06 — GSE167201: Experimental Babesia rossi infection in Beagles, whole-blood RNA-seq time course (gene-level counts and series matrix)

**Why ranked 3:** Whole-blood RNA-seq time course from the same experimental programme (CH09) — enables within-dog integration with DS01/DS02 if dogs can be matched.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Public download verified |
| Linked articles | [CB082](articles/CB082_2021_experimental-babesia-rossi-infection-induces-hemolytic.md) |
| Correspondence check | Corresponds. Series matrix gives PubMed ID 34399690 (the PMID in note CB082), title "Experimental Babesia rossi infection induces hemolytic, metabolic, and viral response pathways in the canine host", and "Six purpose bred male beagles" at Onderstepoort (matches the note). All 28 samples are Beagle, male, whole venous blood, Illumina NovaSeq 6000, TruSeq Stranded mRNA, Tempus tubes. Sampling days are D0, D1, D3, D4, D6, D8, as in the note. In the counts file the post-baseline series covers 5 dogs: low_1 and low_2 (6 days each), high_1 and high_2 (6 days each), and high_3 (D0, D1, D3 only). That matches the note's 2 low-dose and 3 high-dose infected dogs and the one high-dose death on day 4. Discrepancies: (a) the GEO series summary and overall design say 3 low-dose and 2 high-dose dogs, with high dose 10^9; the note and the counts file give 2 low and 3 high (note gives 10^8, itself inconsistent with 10^9 in the text). (b) The matrix has a sixth ID, low_0, with a single D0 sample and labelled dose: low. The note says six Beagles were used (five infected plus one splenectomised donor), so low_0 may be the sixth dog, but the files do not say so. (c) The GEO data-processing text says reads were mapped to the "mm10 mouse reference genome and Gencode M25", but the genome build field says CanFam3.1 and the gene IDs are Ensembl CanFam (ENSCAFG). The mouse text looks like a metadata copy-paste error, but it is not resolvable from the files. |
| Repository / accession | NCBI GEO (BioProject PRJNA703846; raw FASTQ in SRA, not downloaded) / GSE167201 (platform GPL25760; sample accessions GSM5098925 to GSM5098952; article DOI 10.1186/s12864-021-07889-4) |
| Landing page | https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/query/acc.cgi?acc=GSE167201 |
| Licence / reuse terms | Not specified at dataset level. No dataset-specific licence is shown in the series matrix; the repository's general terms apply (NCBI/GEO data-use policies, no login or acceptance needed to download; I did not check the live NCBI policy page). The linked article PMC8369750 is CC BY according to the Europe PMC record "license" field (queried 2026-10-23). That licence covers the article, not necessarily the GEO data. |
| Species | Canis lupus familiaris (taxid 9615), purpose-bred male Beagles; Babesia rossi experimental infection |
| Design | Controlled experimental infection with B. rossi at two inoculum doses (low about 10^4 parasites, high about 10^8-10^9). Longitudinal whole-blood mRNA-seq on days 0, 1, 3, 4, 6 and 8. Diminazene aceturate was given on day 4 per the GEO summary and article note, so D6 and D8 samples are post-treatment. The note also says transfusion was given from day 4; this is not in the files. No parallel uninfected control group; D0 pre-inoculation baseline only. |
| Granularity | individual dog/sample-level, longitudinal, processed omics |
| Raw vs processed | Processed gene-level count matrix: RSEM expected counts from STAR alignment to CanFam3.1, stored as integers (no non-integer values; presumably rounded). It is not raw reads (FASTQ is in SRA, not downloaded). The matrix is already filtered: only 12,362 genes are present, with no all-zero genes, consistent with the stated CPM filter. Unfiltered gene-level counts are therefore not available from this file. Normalisation factors are not provided. |
| Independent animals | 6 — From counts-file column names (dose_dogID_day): 6 distinct dog IDs (low_0, low_1, low_2, high_1, high_2, high_3). Five of them have a post-baseline time course; low_0 has only D0. This agrees with GEO sample titles. Dog IDs are numbered within dose group, so low_1 and high_1 are different animals. The identity and role of low_0 (a sixth animal, possibly the donor) is unverified. GEO text (3 low, 2 high) conflicts with the column labels (2 low, 3 high with time courses); the labels and the article note agree with each other. |
| Samples | 28 |
| Groups and controls | Two dose groups. Low dose: 2 dogs with time courses (low_1, low_2) plus low_0 with a D0 sample only. High dose: 3 dogs (high_1, high_2 with D0 to D8; high_3 with D0, D1, D3 only, because it died on day 4 per the note). No uninfected or untreated parallel control; each dog's D0 sample is its own baseline. Samples per time point: D0 6, D1 5, D3 5, D4 4, D6 4, D8 4 (total 28). Sample counts per dog: 6, 6, 6, 6, 3, 1. |
| Time points | Days post-inoculation 0, 1, 3, 4, 6, 8 (GEO series text also notes day 0 as the pre-inoculation baseline). The article describes daily clinical sampling, but only these six days are sequenced and present. Diminazene on day 4; the D4 sample timing relative to dosing is not stated in the files. |
| Features | 12,362 genes (rows) by 28 samples (columns). Row IDs are composite "ENSCAFG########_SYMBOL" strings; 1,922 rows have no symbol (the symbol slot repeats the Ensembl ID). Counts are integers, with 1.8% zeros and no missing values. Library sizes (sum of counts over the 12,362 genes) are about 30 to 44 million in most samples. They collapse to about 7.2M (high_1 D3), 4.2M (high_2 D3), 4.3M (high_3 D3) and 1.3M and 0.8M (high_1 and high_2 D4), and to about 15.5M and 8.9M (low_1 and low_2 D4). Erythroid and reticulocyte transcripts (e.g. ALAS2) rise in those samples. This is a composition and technical effect, so normalisation is critical. Sample-level metadata in the series matrix: breed, sex (all Beagle and male, so no variation), dose (high or low), day, dog number (in the title), and the GSM accession. No clinical or laboratory variables are in these files (the article's haematology, biochemistry and Luminex cytokine data are not deposited here). |
| Outcomes | None as labels. Only dose group, day and, indirectly, death (high_3 stops after D3). No severity score, parasitaemia, haematocrit, survival variable or outcome label is in the files. Dose can serve as a severity proxy, as the article says onset and severity scale with dose, but that is a design factor rather than an observed outcome. |
| Data dictionary | Absent as a separate file. Sample identity is encoded in the column names (dose_dog_Dday) and mapped to GSM IDs and titles in the series matrix. Units are counts (RSEM expected counts); the description field only says "raw_counts.csv". No gene annotation table is supplied beyond the embedded Ensembl ID and symbol. The series matrix expression table is empty (0 rows per sample); data are in the supplementary file only. The processing text contains an obvious mouse-genome error. |
| Missingness | Counts: 0 missing values (0 of 346,136 cells); 1.8% zeros; no all-zero genes. Design: unbalanced and incomplete. low_0 has 1 of 6 time points, high_3 has 3 of 6, and 4 dogs have all 6. Sequenced days only (no D2, D5, D7). Metadata (breed and sex) is constant, with no variation. Clinical variables are absent entirely. |
| Technical requirements | Any scripting environment able to read a tab-delimited gzipped matrix (R or Python); about 1.7 MB uncompressed. Differential-expression workflows use edgeR, limma-voom or DESeq2 (the authors used edgeR). Gene annotation requires Ensembl CanFam3.1 (ENSCAFG IDs); mapping to human orthologues needs Ensembl BioMart or similar for cross-species work. Raw reads would need the SRA toolkit and about 28 paired-end 100 bp NovaSeq libraries for realignment; these were not downloaded. Authors' code is reported at GitHub rlsmith1/Babesia_RNAseq (taken from the article note; not checked). |
| Files | GSE167201_raw_counts.txt.gz: 688,810 bytes (1,679,900 bytes uncompressed), tab-delimited, 12,363 rows including header by 29 columns (symbol plus 28 samples). GSE167201_series_matrix.txt.gz: 4,275 bytes (70,575 uncompressed), GEO SOFT-like metadata, 28 samples, empty expression table. SHA-256 values are in data/manifest.jsonl (counts b2575d138ca08fb7..., series matrix 4e2917a96927162e...). Both files were retrieved 2026-10-23 and read in full without being executed. |
| Overlaps | All samples come from one study (CB082) and share the same dogs across days, so they are repeated measures: 28 samples from 6 dogs. Samples within a dog are not independent, and dog identity is confounded with dose group. The same experimental animals may underlie other publications from this group; the article note says overlap with earlier publications is not reported. The clinical and cytokine data in the article are not in this dataset. Whether any other dataset in this review shares these dogs is unverified. |

**Downloaded files (2; 0.69 MB):** `data/raw/DS06/GSE167201_raw_counts.txt.gz` (688,810 B, sha256 `b2575d138ca0…`); `data/raw/DS06/GSE167201_series_matrix.txt.gz` (4,275 B, sha256 `4e2917a96927…`)

**Suitable questions:** Within-dog temporal host transcriptional response to B. rossi (D0 baseline versus D1, D3, D4), including interferon and antiviral signatures, haemolysis and erythropoiesis (reticulocyte and erythroid transcripts) and innate-immune pathways. Descriptive comparison of low versus high dose trajectories. Estimating how whole-blood composition shifts (deconvolution) drive apparent expression change. Reuse as a hypothesis-generating reference for comparison with natural-infection or human severe malaria and babesiosis signatures. Not suitable for outcome prediction (no outcome labels), for clinical biomarker development, or for claims about natural tick-borne disease.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are more defensible than ML. Use count models with dog as a random effect or blocking factor: limma-voom with duplicateCorrelation or dream, edgeR/DESeq2 with a paired design (~dog + day), and time-course models such as splines only descriptively. Normalise with TMM or similar and check sample-level QC; the collapsed library sizes at D3 and D4 suggest erythroid and globin dominance, so consider composition-aware normalisation and sensitivity analyses with and without those samples. Report pathway enrichment as exploratory (gene-set tests such as camera or fry that account for inter-gene correlation). If any predictive modelling is attempted, restrict it to leave-one-dog-out validation with penalised regression, and treat it as a demonstration only. Do not interpret clusters of genes or samples as subtypes.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n and high p: 5 dogs with time courses (2 low, 3 high) plus a baseline-only dog, against 12,362 genes, so power is very low and dose effects cannot be separated from individual-dog effects (dose and dog are fully nested). Repeated measures: 28 samples are not independent, so all splits must be at the animal level (leave-one-dog-out); sample-level cross-validation would leak. Treatment confounding: diminazene on day 4 (and, per the note, transfusion from day 4) means D4 to D8 reflect treatment, recovery and transfusion rather than untreated infection; there is no untreated control arm. The death of high_3 on day 4 introduces survivor bias after D3 (informative missingness). No parallel uninfected controls, so time effects are confounded with handling, repeated phlebotomy and tube storage. Technical: collapsed library sizes at D3 and D4 and strong erythroid and reticulocyte composition shifts (whole-blood RNA with globin and erythroid content) make normalisation sensitive; batch and library-preparation information is not provided, and day may be confounded with sequencing batch (unverified). Outcome leakage: no outcome labels exist, but any severity proxy such as dose or day is the design variable itself. Metadata errors: GEO text says 3 low and 2 high dogs, 10^9 parasites, and mm10 mapping, which conflicts with the counts file, the article note and CanFam3.1 gene IDs. The role of low_0 is unclear. The count matrix is pre-filtered and integer-rounded, so the full gene set and the original expected counts are not available. No clinical or laboratory variables are included, so phenotype anchoring needs the article's supplementary or other sources. Cross-species comparability: Beagles under experimental, specific-pathogen-controlled conditions with a passaged inoculum (possibly transfusion-like rather than tick-borne), a single sex and a single breed, so extrapolation to naturally infected client-owned dogs, or to human infections, is limited; orthologue mapping adds noise.

**Unverified fields:** Identity and role of low_0 (donor or sixth dog, or a mislabelled sample); Which of the conflicting dose/dog numbers is right (GEO text says 3 low and 2 high; counts file and note say 2 low and 3 high), and high inoculum size (10^8 vs 10^9); Whether high_3 died on day 4 (inferred from missing D4 to D8 samples plus the note; not stated in the files); Timing of diminazene and any transfusion relative to the D4 sample (taken from the note, not from the files); Sequencing batch and library-preparation batch per sample; Whether the mm10 mouse mapping statement in the GEO processing text is an error (inferred from CanFam3.1 and ENSCAFG IDs); Reason for collapsed library sizes at D3 and D4 (erythroid and globin composition is suggested by ALAS2, SLC4A1 and GYPA counts but not tested); Dataset-specific licence or terms (only the general GEO terms are assumed; the live NCBI policy page was not checked); Existence and content of GitHub code rlsmith1/Babesia_RNAseq (from the note only); Content and availability of the SRA FASTQ files (not downloaded); Whether the counts are rounded RSEM expected counts (inferred from the all-integer values and the GEO file description)

**Why ranked 4:** Serum TMT proteome of 52 dogs with control/uncomplicated/complicated labels; RAW data also public.

(1) sample_list.xlsx lists 52 unique dog IDs: 20 controls (1z-20z), 18 uncomplicated and 14 complicated babesiosis (numeric IDs), matching the 52 dogs / 20 / 18 / 14 in the CB112 note and the PRIDE record. (2) B_Proteins.txt has 224 master proteins; 128 of these have at least 2 unique peptides, equal to the 128 proteins quantified in the article note (the 128 is a filter I applied and matched, not a label in the file). (3) Species is Canis lupus familiaris (all 224 master descriptions OX=9615); TMT sixplex with an internal standard in channel 126, Sequest HT in Proteome Discoverer, matching the PRIDE protocol text (PD 2.3, Q Exactive Plus, UniProt Canis FASTA of 14 Oct 2021, 49,889 sequences). (4) The PRIDE API (read 2026-10-23) lists the CB112 reference (PMID 37353646, DOI 10.1038/s41598-023-37312-9) and the same group sizes and complicated-disease criteria. The manifest's linked_articles field for these files is empty, so linkage rests on the PRIDE record and the content match above. |

| Design | Retrospective single-time-point case-control TMT-sixplex shotgun proteomics of archived serum. Three groups: healthy controls, uncomplicated B. rossi babesiosis, complicated B. rossi babesiosis. Complicated disease was defined by collapse, cerebral signs, death within 24 h, or abnormal clinical pathology thresholds (PRIDE protocol text). 11 TMT sixplex runs (B1-B11), each with a pooled internal standard in channel 126 and 5 randomised samples in channels 127-131; B11 has only 2 samples (channels 129-131 unused). Group composition per plex is roughly balanced (about 2 control, 1-2 complicated, 1-3 uncomplicated), which limits plex-group confounding but does not remove plex effects. |
| Granularity | individual dog/sample-level, processed omics |
| Raw vs processed | Processed only. Downloaded: Proteome Discoverer 2.3 protein-level exports with per-sample TMT reporter abundances (scaled to the internal standard = 100) and IS-ratio columns, a presence/absence protein-group table, and a plain-sheet sample layout. Not downloaded: 11 RAW files (about 800 MB each, about 8.8 GB total), 11 MGF peak files (about 170-210 MB each) and B_PeptideGroups.txt (1.6 MB), all listed in the PRIDE file list. So the full dataset is about 11 GB, and the peptide-level table could be fetched cheaply if peptide-level analysis is needed. |
| Independent animals | 52 — Counted from sample_list.xlsx: 52 unique IDs (20 control 'z' IDs, 14 complicated, 18 uncomplicated), each appearing exactly once across the 11 plex layouts (52 IDs, 52 unique, no ID missing or duplicated). Matches the 52 sample channels (S12-S63) in the PD exports. Dogs equal samples, since there is one serum per dog and no repeated measures. One uncertainty is that the group-to-ID assignment comes from sample_list.xlsx column layout, which I read through the read-only helper. |
| Samples | 52 |
| Groups and controls | Controls n=20 (healthy, 6 female / 14 male, median age 21 months, range 12-120, per PRIDE text); uncomplicated babesiosis n=18; complicated babesiosis n=14. The healthy controls are a genuine comparator, but they were not matched on age (infected median 28 months per the CB112 note) and no sex or breed data are in the files. Group sizes are not balanced against the 11 plexes exactly, but each plex has controls and cases. |
| Time points | Single time point per dog (admission or archived serum, retrospective). The time of sampling relative to treatment is not stated in the files, so pre-treatment status is unverified. No longitudinal data. |
| Features | B_Proteins.txt: 677 rows x 206 columns, of which 224 are IsMasterProtein rows (the unit of analysis), with 235 master candidates and 218 non-master rows. Columns: accession (UniProt), description incl. gene symbol, q-value, Sum PEP score, coverage %, #peptides, #PSMs, #unique peptides, #AAs, MW kDa, pI, Sequest score; 55 IS-ratio columns (5 channels x 11 plexes, relative to channel 126); 66 'Abundances (Grouped)' columns (6 channels x 11 plexes, IS channel = 100 by construction); 66 'Found in Sample' High / Not Found flags. B_ProteinGroups.txt: 224 groups x 73 columns, with peptide / PSM counts and Found-in-Sample flags only, no abundances. Useful quantified set: 217 master proteins have at least 1 value, 156 have at least 26 of 52, 131 have at least 39 of 52, 95 have all 52, and 128 have at least 2 unique peptides. Median 149-158 proteins detected per sample. |
| Outcomes | Three-class label only (control / uncomplicated / complicated), recoverable from sample_list.xlsx. No survival, death, length-of-stay or continuous severity score, and no individual clinical-pathology values or parasitaemia in the files. The 'complicated' label is defined partly by clinical pathology variables (haematocrit, urea, creatinine, bilirubin, glucose, lactate, band cells) that are not supplied, so the label cannot be re-derived or adjusted for. |
| Data dictionary | Partial. No data dictionary file. Proteome Discoverer column headers are self-describing (units: MW in kDa, coverage in %), and the sample_list.xlsx sheet contains an informal TMT channel layout and 'IS - internal standard' note. The channel header 'Found in Sample: [Sn] Fm: 12x, Control/Sample' labels only channel 126 as 'Control' (this is the internal standard pool, not a control dog) and the others as 'Sample', which is a trap: PD sample type does not equal biological group. Group membership must be reconstructed by joining plex Fm and TMT channel to the sample_list layout. PRIDE provides the full protocol text (PD 2.3, parameters, database). |
| Missingness | Substantial and not at random. Across 224 master proteins x 52 samples, about 31% of abundance cells are empty (4850 empty of 14784 including 11 IS and 3 unused channels; 31.1% in the 52 sample channels only). Missingness is strongly protein-dependent: 95 proteins complete, 34 proteins complete in the 62-sample... wait-counted table: many proteins missing in nearly all channels (7 proteins with all 66 empty). Restricting to proteins in at least half of samples gives about 9% residual missingness. Missingness follows plex (low-abundance proteins drop out in some runs), so it is plex-structured and likely abundance-dependent (MNAR). Channels F11 129/130/131 are unused but carry spurious values (median about 101-127 in 46-90 proteins), so they must be excluded as noise, not treated as samples. |
| Technical requirements | Plain tab-delimited text (UTF-8) readable in R/Python; sample_list.xlsx is a layout sheet needing a manual or scripted join (no tidy long table). Annotation is UniProt Canis (Sequest HT via Proteome Discoverer 2.3), with many 'Uncharacterized protein' and LOC gene IDs, so gene-symbol mapping needs UniProt/Ensembl or human-ortholog mapping for pathway work. Re-processing from RAW/MGF would need Proteome Discoverer or open tools (MSFragger, Philosopher, TMT-Integrator) and about 11 GB storage, but is not needed for protein-level analysis. IS-ratio abundances are already on a common scale across plexes. |
| Files | Downloaded: sample_list.xlsx (10,175 B; one sheet, TMT layout), B_Proteins.txt (1,032,857 B; 677 x 206), B_ProteinGroups.txt (156,263 B; 224 x 73). Not downloaded (PRIDE listing): 11 x B*_serum.raw (about 780-874 MB each), 11 x B*.mgf (about 167-213 MB each), B_PeptideGroups.txt (1,555,582 B). SHA-256 values are in data/manifest.jsonl. |
| Overlaps | Same dogs as the CB112 article and its supplementary Table 1 (protein list with statistics); no independent second cohort. The CB112 note records no statement on overlap with earlier cohorts from the same Pretoria/Zagreb groups, so dog overlap with other B. rossi studies in this review (e.g. other omics or clinical-pathology datasets) is unverified. No repeated measures within this dataset. Each dog appears once, but the pooled internal standard contains material from all 52 dogs, so the ratios are slightly self-referential (minor). |

**Downloaded files (3; 1.20 MB):** `data/raw/DS07/sample_list.xlsx` (10,175 B, sha256 `3119957fa2b3…`); `data/raw/DS07/B_Proteins.txt` (1,032,857 B, sha256 `2fc5d617dea4…`); `data/raw/DS07/B_ProteinGroups.txt` (156,263 B, sha256 `3633e6db98c7…`)

**Suitable questions:** Which serum proteins (acute-phase, lipoprotein, complement, coagulation / haemostasis, CD14, lactoferrin, ITIH4, APOE, RBP4, transferrin) differ between healthy, uncomplicated and complicated B. rossi babesiosis; effect sizes and rank stability of the candidate severity markers; consistency of the proteomic signature with published clinical-pathology and acute-phase findings; hypothesis generation for pathway-level pathophysiology (inflammation, hypolipidaemia, hypercoagulability, negative acute-phase reactants); a reference proteome for cross-study or cross-species comparison (B. rossi vs other Babesia or human malaria sepsis) at pathway level only.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are more defensible than ML here. Use protein-wise linear models on log2 IS-ratios with plex as a fixed or random effect (limma-style moderated tests or linear mixed models), 3-group omnibus then pairwise contrasts, Benjamini-Hochberg FDR, and exact or permutation tests for rank-based comparisons (Kruskal-Wallis / Mann-Whitney with exact p where feasible). For missing data use detection-aware handling (restrict to proteins observed in at least 50-75% of samples; presence/absence or left-censored models for the rest) rather than blind imputation. For a complicated vs uncomplicated classifier, use penalised regression (elastic net / lasso) or a few pre-specified markers with repeated nested cross-validation and ROC with bootstrap CIs; report as exploratory. Do not train deep or high-capacity ML models on n=32 infected dogs. Cluster or PCA structure should be described as exploratory groupings, never as subtypes.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n / high p: 52 dogs (14 complicated) against 128-217 usable proteins, so any multivariable model is badly under-powered and the selection of 'biomarkers' will be optimistic without nested CV. Batch: 11 TMT plexes; groups are spread across plexes (blocked) but plex adjustment is needed, and only 2 samples in plex 11. Missingness is non-random and plex-linked. Spurious signal in the 3 unused channels of plex 11 must be removed. Treatment and selection confounding: retrospective, treatment status (diminazene, transfusion, fluids) and sampling time not in the files; controls are not age- or sex-matched and are healthy, so control vs infected is trivially separable and is largely an acute-phase/inflammation signal. Outcome leakage: 'complicated' is defined by clinical pathology (haematocrit, urea, creatinine, bilirubin, glucose, lactate) and by death within 24 h, so any proteins that track anaemia, renal injury or hepatic function partly restate the label rather than predict it; no independent outcome (survival) is supplied. Animal-level splitting is trivial here (one sample per dog) but the pooled internal standard and plex membership are shared, so cross-validation should be stratified by plex and group. Validation: no external cohort; the article's Western blot validation covers only 2 proteins with partly discordant results; internal CV only. Cross-species comparability: canine UniProt IDs with many uncharacterised/LOC entries; TMT ratio compression and serum depth (about 128 solid proteins) limit comparison with human or other-species proteomes. No covariates (age, sex, breed, parasitaemia, haematology) in the files, so confounding cannot be adjusted for from this dataset alone. The CB112 supplementary table should be consulted for covariates and for the published statistics.

**Unverified fields:** time_points (sampling time relative to treatment/admission not in files); overlaps (dog overlap with other cohorts and with other datasets in this review); age/sex/breed per dog (not in files; only group-level medians in PRIDE text); exact article n of 128 'quantified' proteins vs my >=2-unique-peptide filter (matches but filter chosen by me); raw/MGF/peptide-group contents (not downloaded, inspected only by PRIDE file listing); whether the sample_list group labels exactly match the article's final group assignment per dog (only group counts verified)

Serum cytokines by multiplex bead assay (Luminex); haematology by automated analyser. |

The zip also contains rendered images (JPG and GIF versions) of the article's Tables 1-4 and Figures 1-8, which are aggregate/summary renderings and not tabular data. |

The ID-to-dog identity relies on ID reuse across the day-1 and day-7 blocks ('BK 1' with a space on day 1 in the cytokine sheet was normalised to BK1). The H vs HI prefixes are not explained in the file; I assumed 16 distinct healthy dogs (consistent with the article, which describes two groups of 8) rather than repeat sampling of 8 dogs. |
| Samples | 77 |
| Groups and controls | Healthy controls n=16 (single sample each; H = 8 and HI = 8, probably the article's subgroups 2a and 2b, mapping unverified; article reports no significant difference between subgroups and merged them). Uncomplicated babesiosis (IDs B1-B13), n=13 at day 1 and day 7. Complicated babesiosis (IDs BK1-BK18), n=18 at day 1, n=17 at day 7 (BK11 lost). Group is encoded only in the ID prefix and in a legend in side cells (B = UNCOMPLICATED, BK = COMPLICATED) and in column A ('Healthy' / 'Diseased'), not in a dedicated group column. No untreated infected arm; all infected dogs were treated. |
| Time points | Infected dogs: day 1 (presentation; cytokine draw 6-8 h after imidocarb per full text) and day 7. Healthy controls: single time point. Day is encoded only by block order and by a text row ('Diseased day 1' / 'Diseased day 7') in the HEMATOLOGY sheet; the CYTOKINES sheet has no day label at all (two repeated 'Diseased' blocks of 31 then 30 rows, order inferred and consistent with n and medians in the article). |

Neut, Seg. Neut, Mono, Eos, Thromb, HMT, Eryt, Lymph %, Nes. Neut %, Seg neut %, Mono %, Eos %, Total gran, n:l). No MODS/SIRS flags, no length of stay. |

A third sheet 'Hoja1' is empty. |
| Missingness | Essentially none in the numeric blocks: 0 blank analyte cells among 77 samples x 9 cytokines; haematology complete for 77 samples x 16 variables. Structural missingness only: BK11 has no day-7 sample (death); healthy dogs have no second time point. Hidden censoring: many values are floor/imputed (IL-10 = 0.8 in 45/77; IL-18 = 2 in 18/77; IL-2 = 10, IL-15 = 12, IL-8 = 10 as minima), and values are heavily quantised (e.g. IL-18 repeats 83, 144.9, 188.4, 225, 257.9). Apparent data-entry errors in haematology: BK9 day 1 erythrocytes = 35 (equal to HMT, implausible); B2 day 7 segmented neutrophils = 42 (probably 4.2); BK12 day 7 segmented neutrophils 1.4 although 59% of WBC 28.3; BK1 day 1 lymphocyte count 0.5 vs 5% of 19 (borderline). |
| Technical requirements | Trivial: open the XLSX with any spreadsheet library (openpyxl/pandas). Needs manual reshaping because the sheets are stacked blocks with in-sheet text labels (rows 'Healthy', 'Diseased', 'Diseased day 1', 'Diseased day 7') and side legends and reference ranges in the same grid; sheet dimensions (355 x 15 and 80 x 27) include mostly empty cells (actual data 77 rows each). Need to build group/day columns from ID prefix and block order, normalise IDs ('BK 1'), and decide treatment of floored values (censored). Use log transforms for cytokines (right-skewed). |
| Files | PMC5756041_supplementaryFiles.zip, 701,776 bytes, SHA-256 c6e1ce1301b3309c982607b5da3b0f97a669cb31d7692284c20e8ba57daf8100, retrieved 2026-10-23. Contains 25 members: pone.0190474.s001.xlsx (85,129 bytes; sheets CYTOKINES 77 data rows, HEMATOLOGY 77 data rows, empty Hoja1) and 24 image files (JPG and GIF renderings of Tables 1-4 and Figures 1-8 of the paper, 7-171 KB each; not parsed as data). |
| Overlaps | The 47 dogs are the same cohort as article CB017 only. Overlap with other cohorts from the same Zagreb group (e.g. other Mrljak/Galán/Cerón studies) is not established. The image members duplicate article tables and figures. The same dog appears at day 1 and day 7 (30 dogs twice), so the 77 rows are not independent. The healthy groups include 8 dogs given prophylactic imidocarb (if H/HI = 2a), a pharmacological exposure not shared with the other healthy dogs. |

**Downloaded files (1; 0.70 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB017/PMC5756041_supplementaryFiles.zip` (701,776 B, sha256 `c6e1ce1301b3…`)

**Suitable questions:** (1) Reproduce and re-test the article claims: day-1 MCP-1 and KC-like in complicated vs uncomplicated; IL-8 rise over time (paired day 1 to day 7) in uncomplicated dogs; IL-8 vs erythrocyte count/HCT correlation within group. (2) Paired day 1 vs day 7 cytokine and neutrophil-left-shift (band count, n:l) kinetics, with the caveat of treatment. (3) Cross-sectional correlation structure between chemokines (KC-like, IL-8, MCP-1) and haematology (anaemia, thrombocytopenia, band neutrophils, leukocytosis/leukopenia) in B. canis dogs, as a comparison source for the B. rossi cytokine literature. (4) Healthy reference intervals for these nine analytes on this multiplex panel (n=16). Not suitable: survival prediction (one death, unclear 'Dead' label), subtype discovery, causal/mechanistic inference, anything needing parasitaemia, biochemistry, organ-dysfunction scores or clinical covariates.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are more defensible than ML. Use dog-level units: exact or Mann-Whitney / permutation tests (with multiplicity control) for between-group comparisons on day 1 and day 7 separately; Wilcoxon signed-rank or linear mixed models with a random dog intercept (log-transformed cytokines, group x day interaction) for longitudinal analyses; Spearman correlations with bootstrap CIs within group; Tobit/censored or sensitivity analyses for floored analytes (IL-10, IL-18, IL-2) or simply dichotomise them; for prediction of complicated disease, a small penalised logistic regression (ridge/elastic net, 1-3 pre-specified markers such as MCP-1, IL-7) with repeated nested cross-validation or leave-one-dog-out, reporting optimism-corrected AUC with bootstrap CI and calibration. Any split must be by dog (day-1 and day-7 rows of the same dog must stay together; day 7 should not be used to predict complication status). Avoid clustering/subtype claims; with n=31 any cluster is exploratory.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n (47 dogs, 18 vs 13 in the key contrast, one death) with 25 features, so small-n/high-p behaviour for any multi-marker model; the article's AUC 0.983 for combined markers is apparent (resubstitution) and is not externally validated; no independent validation cohort in the file. Outcome leakage and label definition: 'complicated' was defined by WHO malaria-style criteria with SIRS-positive dogs (and 66% MODS positive), so inflammatory markers and WBC (a SIRS criterion) partly define the label; haematology variables (WBC, HCT) are themselves label-defining or label-correlated; day-7 samples are post-treatment and post-complication-assignment and must not be used to predict day-1 class. Treatment confounding: every infected dog received imidocarb and supportive care (infusion, anti-inflammatories, transfusion if HCT <20%), and cytokine draws were 6-8 h post-imidocarb, while 8 healthy dogs apparently also received prophylactic imidocarb; no untreated infected comparator and no treatment dose/timing, transfusion or anti-inflammatory variables in the file. Selection: retrospective, single centre, recruitment period unstated, exclusion of dogs with concurrent disease, healthy controls from a different recruitment route (prophylaxis visitors/routine checks), with no age/sex/breed in the file, so group comparisons may be confounded. Batch: no plate/batch/date column; healthy sub-groups H and HI may have been run in different batches (article says no differences). Measurement: concentrations are interpolated and heavily quantised with imputed floors (IL-10 mostly 0.8), which breaks distributional assumptions; the unlabelled 'Dead' flag (9 dogs) conflicts with the one reported death and is unresolved; haematology contains apparent entry errors (BK9 erythrocytes 35, B2 day-7 segmented neutrophils 42, BK12 day-7 count inconsistent) and units are implied only. Repeated measures: 30 dogs contribute two non-independent rows. Cross-species comparability: assay is a canine multiplex panel with 'KC-like' (canine IL-8 homologue ambiguity: both KC-like and IL-8 are reported); absolute pg/ml are platform-specific and not comparable with human or other canine panels (e.g., B. rossi studies) without calibration; comparison with B. rossi is across studies and assays, and the file includes only B. canis.

**Unverified fields:** meaning of H vs HI sample prefixes (assumed to map to article groups 2a/2b); meaning of the unlabelled 'Dead' column (9 complicated dogs at day 1, 8 at day 7) versus the single death reported; day labelling of CYTOKINES sheet (inferred from block order and article medians); identity of BK11 as the dog that died (inferred from missing day-7 row); identity of repeated dogs across day 1 and day 7 (assumed from reused IDs); units of haematology columns (inferred from reference-range block); dataset-specific licence for S1 Table (inherited from the article CC BY licence); meaning of below-detection floors/imputation rules (inferred from repeated minimum values); whether sampling recruitment overlaps other cohorts from the same group; content of image members (not opened as data; identified only by file names)

No separate data dictionary or README: abbreviations (ARC, CHCM, CHr, CHDWr, RDWr, CHDelta, MCVr, CHCMr) are not defined, analyser model and reference ranges absent, sex coding is plain text, cohort definitions and Day 1-6 meaning are not defined. Minor inconsistencies: CHDWr unit is 'pg/cell' in the natural/IMHA headers and 'pg' in the experimental units row. Sheet2/Sheet3 are empty. Dates of sampling absent (good for privacy, limits batch assessment). |
| Missingness | Natural + controls (117 dogs): complete 107 rows. Weight missing in 8, age missing in 2, sex in 1 (BLSC9 lacks age, weight and sex), MCVr, CHCMr, CHDWr, RDWr and CHDelta missing in 2 dogs (BC02, BC22, which also lack several reticulocyte indices). IMHA (16): weight missing 7/16; MCVr, CHCMr, CHr, CHDWr, RDWr and CHDelta missing in 5 (H04, H05, H09, H10, H14); only 6 of 16 rows fully complete. Experimental: no missing values in dogs 1-4; dog 5 lacks Days 5-6 for all 12 variables (12 x 2 = 24 of 360 cells, about 6.7%). Implausible or artefactual values are present, e.g. exp case 5 MCHC Day 4 = 595 g/L, IMHA MCHC up to 478, and BC24 RDWr 1.6, CHDWr 1.0, which need checking and are not annotated. |
| Technical requirements | None special. Standard xlsx readers (openpyxl, pandas, R readxl). Natural/IMHA files are long format with section header rows (cohort labels in column A, which must be skipped); the experimental file is wide with a title row and a units row (header on row 2, units on row 3), and must be reshaped to long format (dog x day) for mixed models. Numeric cells are stored with float noise (e.g. 1.0900000000000001) and need rounding. Total size under 50 kB. |
| Files | 3 xlsx files, each with Sheet1 populated and Sheet2/Sheet3 empty: 'Babesia Retics_Natural inf and cont_dissertation.xlsx' (24,257 bytes, 121 rows x 17 cols, 117 data rows plus 3 section/header rows), 'Babesia Retics_IMHA_dissertation.xlsx' (12,501 bytes, 18 rows x 17 cols, 16 dogs), 'Babesia Retics_ Exp infected dissertation.xlsx' (12,815 bytes, 8 rows x 73 cols, 5 dogs x 6 days). SHA-256 in data/manifest.jsonl. Retrieved 2026-10-23. |
| Overlaps | Corresponds to CB115 (probable). Dogs in the natural cohorts probably also appear in other University of Pretoria B. rossi studies from the same hospital and research group (e.g. other CB entries on B. rossi anaemia or haematology), but no overlap can be confirmed from the files; IDs are study-specific (BC, BLSC, CON, H). Experimental dogs may come from an earlier B. rossi challenge study (unverified). Repeated measures only within the 5 experimental dogs; all other rows are one sample per dog. |

**Downloaded files (3; 0.05 MB):** `data/raw/DS03/Babesia Retics_IMHA_dissertation.xlsx` (12,501 B, sha256 `410929f2b5f9…`); `data/raw/DS03/Babesia Retics_Natural inf and cont_dissertation.xlsx` (24,257 B, sha256 `0a3e3e74835c…`); `data/raw/DS03/Babesia Retics_ Exp infected dissertation.xlsx` (12,815 B, sha256 `5f0d44439ef3…`)

**Suitable questions:** (1) Marrow response vs anaemia severity in natural B. rossi: ARC, Ret% and reticulocyte indices (MCVr, CHr, CHCMr, CHDWr, RDWr) against HGB/HCT, in infected dogs vs controls vs IMHA (reticulocyte response adequacy, e.g. ARC relative to HCT). (2) Reticulocyte morphology differences (macrocytic, hypochromic, heterogeneous) between B. rossi, IMHA and control dogs. (3) Experimental time course: shape of the ARC rise and collapse over 6 days in 4-5 dogs, descriptive only, with hypothesis generation about early marrow suppression. (4) Reference-style data on reticulocyte indices in healthy dogs (n = 14, small). Not suitable for: prognosis/severity prediction (no outcome labels), parasitaemia relationships (absent), treatment-effect or recovery claims (treatment timing not recorded), omics-style integration.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are more defensible than ML. Group comparisons with Kruskal-Wallis/Wilcoxon or robust regression, adjusting for age and sex (ANCOVA/linear model of log ARC on HCT x group); beta or logistic regression with exact tests for small groups; multiple-testing control across the correlated index family; PCA only descriptively. Experimental days: linear mixed model with random dog intercept (and possibly slope) or simple within-dog paired plots; with n = 5, report individual trajectories and avoid inferential overreach. If any predictive modelling is attempted (e.g. B. rossi vs IMHA from indices), use penalised logistic regression with nested cross-validation, stratified and at dog level, treating results as exploratory; external validation is not available.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n overall (5 experimental dogs, 16 IMHA, 14 controls) but low p (12 correlated variables), so classical statistics are feasible while ML is overkill. The 12 variables are strongly collinear and partly derived from each other (ARC from Ret% and RBC; CHDelta from CHr; MCHC/CHCM/CHCMr co-vary with HGB/HCT), so feature importance is not interpretable and any model that uses HCT/HGB to classify against controls or defines the regenerative response from ARC would leak the phenotype. Selection and confounding: IMHA and natural cases are hospital admissions (selection by severity, unknown time since onset, prior treatment or transfusion not recorded); controls are older and mostly female whereas infected dogs are young and mostly male; the age confound with reticulocyte indices is substantial. Two natural cohorts of unknown difference (possible batch or period effect; analyser calibration/dates not given); no batch information. IMHA group n differs from the article (16 vs 10), so analyses may not reproduce published numbers. No outcome/severity labels, no treatment or parasitaemia data; 'Day' in the experimental file is not anchored to events; dog 5's missing Days 5-6 may reflect informative dropout (death/euthanasia), biasing late-day estimates. Repeated measures exist only in the experimental dogs, so splitting must be by dog, not by sample; no train/test validation is realistic. Cross-species comparability is limited: canine ADVIA reticulocyte indices and thresholds are not equivalent to human or murine measures; experimental infection used B. rossi in an experimental-strain context that may differ from natural infections. Some implausible values (e.g. MCHC 595, RDWr 1.6) need review. Exploratory structure in the data (clusters of ARC response) must not be called subtypes.

**Unverified fields:** corresponds_to_articles (article full text unavailable; IMHA n 16 vs 10 unresolved; no formal link in the record); licence (taken from supplied metadata, not read from landing page); n_animals independence across files (possible dog overlap not stated); meaning of 'Study cohort 1/2' labels; meaning of experimental Day 1-6 (inoculation/parasitaemia/treatment); reason dog 5 has no Days 5-6; analyser model, reference intervals and sampling dates; treatment/transfusion status of natural cases and IMHA dogs; babesia species confirmation method for natural cases; reason 6 IMHA dogs lack all reticulocyte indices (5 rows) or why n = 10 in the article

### DS05 — Prevalence of Ehrlichia canis or Theileria equi co-infections in dogs that are naturally infected and clinically sick due to babesiosis (BET Study Dataset.xlsx)

**Why ranked 7:** 150 naturally infected dogs (B. rossi) with admission clinical exam, CBC, reticulocytes, proteins and co-infection PCR; no outcome label.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Public download verified |
| Linked articles | [CB126](articles/CB126_2024_prevalence-co-infections-ehrlichia-spp-or.md) |
| Correspondence check | Corresponds to CB126 (Cloete et al. 2024, Vet Parasitol Reg Stud Rep 54:101092), checked against the abstract-level note only, because full text and supplement were not available. Entire-population sheet has 150 unique dog IDs (BET001-BET151; BET073 absent), matching 150 dogs. qPCR flags: B. rossi=1 in 149, B. vogeli=1 in 1 (BET100), E. canis=1 in 3 (BET023, BET072, BET147; all B. rossi co-infected), T. equi=0 in all 150. This matches 149 B. rossi, 1 B. vogeli, 3/149 E. canis and no Theileria. Collection dates run 2021-01-11 to 2021-11-23, matching a single-year (2021) single-clinic design. Variable families match the abstract (monocyte, platelet, band neutrophil and reticulocyte indices, season, demography). Eastern Cape/Mdantsane location is not stated in the file and is taken from the article note. Dataset title and DOI also match the article subject. Not verified: the full-text Methods and any supplement. |
