| Species | Domestic dog (Canis lupus familiaris), natural infection, Babesia rossi (149) and B. vogeli (1); E. canis co-infection in 3. Eastern Cape, South Africa per the article note. |
| Design | Cross-sectional single-clinic cohort of clinically sick dogs with natural babesiosis, with one admission sample per dog in 2021. No babesiosis-negative controls, no treatment data, no follow-up or outcome. Used for co-infection prevalence and for haematology/biochemistry association analysis (including correspondence analysis, per the CORRESPONDENCE sheet). |
| Granularity | individual dog/sample-level |
| Raw vs processed | Mostly observed values. 'Entire population' holds per-dog observed signalment, clinical exam, CBC, differential, reticulocytes, serum proteins, qPCR flags and date. 'CORRESPONDENCE' is derived: the same dogs re-coded into categorical levels (e.g. Ht_VS, ARC_Ma, WCC_Lo) for correspondence analysis, plus a few raw values. It also carries a mental-status (habitus) grade that is not a column in the main sheet. 'Crossbreed vs Purebreed' is a re-sorted copy of the same 150 dogs with free-text lymph-node descriptions. No raw qPCR Cq values and no raw sequences or omics. |
| Independent animals | 150 — 150 unique BET IDs in the 'Entire population' sheet, with zero duplicates (BET001-BET151, BET073 absent). The CORRESPONDENCE and Crossbreed sheets also have 150 unique IDs each, so they are the same dogs. Some IDs carry free-text annotations such as '(B. rossi + E. canis)'. One row per dog, so there are no repeated measures. Whether the same owner or dog was ever re-presented under a new ID cannot be verified. |
| Samples | 150 |
| Groups and controls | No healthy or babesiosis-negative controls. Infection groups: B. rossi only 146, B. rossi + E. canis 3, B. vogeli only 1 (CORRESPONDENCE codes Br_O, Br_Ec, Bv_O). The comparison group for co-infection is only 3 dogs. Other strata are season (Winter 52, Spring 42, Autumn 36, Summer 20), sex (male intact 82, female intact 61, female spayed 5, male castrated 2) and breed (crossbreed 90, pitbull 25, Boerboel 15, others small). The data contain no severity group labels beyond derived categories. |
| Time points | Single admission time point per dog (date collected 2021-01-11 to 2021-11-23, DD/MM/YYYY, 99 distinct dates). No longitudinal or post-treatment sampling. |
| Features | About 41 columns in the main sheet. Signalment: sex, breed, age (months), weight (kg), primary complaint (coded, e.g. IA, L, WL, HgUr, V), duration of illness (days). Clinical exam: temperature (C), pulse, respiratory rate (some entered as 'P+', panting), mucous membranes (free text), lymph-node score 0-2, tick presence 0/1. CBC (13): haemoglobin g/L, RCC, haematocrit L/L, MCV, MCH, MCHC, RCDW, WCC, segmented/band neutrophils, lymphocytes, monocytes, eosinophils, basophils, 'other', platelets, NRBC/100WBC. Reticulocytes: reticulocyte % and ARC. Biochemistry: total serum protein, albumin, globulin (g/L). qPCR flags (4, binary): B. rossi, B. vogeli, E. canis, T. equi. Date and season. The CORRESPONDENCE sheet adds habitus grade and category codes for anaemia, WCC, band count, left shift, monocytes, ARC, globulin, age, breed group and duration. |
| Outcomes | None. There is no survival, discharge, transfusion or complicated/uncomplicated label. Derived severity proxies only: anaemia grade from haematocrit, habitus grade, ARC regeneration category, left-shift flag. Co-infection (E. canis) is the only 'outcome' in the study design. |
| Data dictionary | Partial. Units and reference ranges sit in the second header row of the main sheet. The CORRESPONDENCE sheet has a legend of category codes and cut-offs (e.g. ARC <80, 80-150, 150-300, >300; WCC <6, 6-15, >15). Abbreviations are not defined in a separate document: complaint codes IA/L/WL/HgUr/V/D, 'P+', 'PMG' and the habitus 1+ to 4+ scale. There is no README. MCH, RCDW, reticulocyte % and ARC are marked 'NRR' (no reference range). The date format is not stated, and DD/MM/YYYY was inferred. |
| Missingness | Low. Of 150 dogs, 2 (BET065, BET066) have no CBC or reticulocyte values (all columns 13-31 blank). Serum protein, albumin and globulin are missing in 6 dogs (BET065, 066, 092, 108, 109, 119). Signalment, exam and qPCR columns are complete. Respiratory rate is non-numeric in 24 dogs ('P+'). Duration of illness is 'UNKNOWN' in at least some dogs in the CORRESPONDENCE sheet. At least one implausible value is present (globulin 1 g/L for BET021 in the CORRESPONDENCE sheet), so data cleaning is needed. |
| Technical requirements | Minimal. One xlsx file, readable with openpyxl, pandas or R readxl. Cleaning needed: strip headers, convert numeric text, recode 'P+', free-text mucous membranes, complaint codes and lymph-node text, and parse DD/MM/YYYY dates. No omics tooling needed. |
| Files | One file, 'BET Study Dataset.xlsx', 133,079 bytes, SHA-256 d2aec8e8b369ed0dda3666841c774ce172ec249faa3ea59668ad41521c4f12cf. Three sheets: 'Entire population' (150 dogs x 41 columns plus a Season column, with 2 header rows), 'CORRESPONDENCE' (150 dogs, category-coded, about 31 columns), 'Crossbreed vs Purebreed' (150 dogs, about 35 columns, re-sorted copy with free-text lymph-node findings). Sheet dimensions reported by the structure summary (1000 rows) include blank rows. |
| Overlaps | All three sheets contain the same 150 dogs, so they must not be counted as separate cohorts. The dataset is the source for CB126 only. Any other Cloete/Schoeman Eastern Cape babesiosis publications could reuse the same dogs, but this was not checked. |

**Downloaded files (1; 0.13 MB):** `data/raw/DS05/BET Study Dataset.xlsx` (133,079 B, sha256 `d2aec8e8b369…`)

**Suitable questions:** Descriptive characterisation of the admission clinical-pathology phenotype of natural B. rossi babesiosis in 146 to 149 dogs: anaemia grade, regenerative response (ARC), leukocyte and left-shift patterns, thrombocytopenia, globulin and albumin. Associations of signalment, breed (crossbreed vs purebreed) and season with these variables. Relations among haematology variables, such as reticulocyte response vs anaemia, or the monocyte and band neutrophil patterns. Reference distributions for B. rossi cases in South Africa. It cannot address: co-infection effects (only 3 E. canis dogs), B. vogeli (1 dog), T. equi (0), outcome prediction, treatment response or comparison with healthy or other-disease dogs.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are more defensible than ML. Use descriptive summaries with confidence intervals, exact (Fisher) or Firth penalised tests for co-infection (3 events), and linear or ordinal regression or GLMs with robust SEs for continuous and graded outcomes (e.g. log ARC vs haematocrit, breed and season). Use penalised regression with nested cross-validation only if a modelling goal exists, and stay with a few pre-specified covariates. Treat the CORRESPONDENCE-sheet categories as exploratory and do not call the clusters subtypes. Add multiplicity control. Multiple imputation or complete-case analysis is acceptable for under 5% missingness.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n (150) with about 35 correlated predictors, and effectively n=3 for co-infection, so power is very low and ML would overfit. No true outcome or severity label: anaemia, habitus and ARC categories are derived from the same variables that would be predictors, so a model of one from the others leaks. No controls, so there is no disease-specific signal. Single clinic, single year, self-selected referral population (selection bias and likely hospital case-mix). No treatment or post-treatment data, but admission samples may follow prior treatment, and duration of illness is owner-reported. Each dog has one row, so no repeated-measures or leakage issue, though any split should be by dog and the three sheets are copies of the same dogs. No batch or analyser metadata. Breed categories are imprecise (crossbreed 60%). Season is confounded with the date. Only B. rossi (n=149) is represented, which limits generalisation to other Babesia species. Cross-species comparability to human malaria or other models is indirect, since this is naturally infected dogs with reference ranges quoted in the sheet. No external validation set is provided.

**Unverified fields:** Licence (taken from the known metadata, not re-read from the live record); Full-text correspondence with CB126 (only abstract-level note available; Eastern Cape/Mdantsane location not in the file); Meaning of complaint codes (IA, L, WL, HgUr, V, D), 'P+' and 'PMG', and habitus grade definitions (no data dictionary); Date format (DD/MM/YYYY inferred); Source of the mental-status (habitus) grade in CORRESPONDENCE, which is absent from the main sheet; Whether the same dogs appear in other publications; Whether CORRESPONDENCE values such as globulin 1 g/L (BET021) are data errors; Treatment or prior-treatment status of dogs at sampling; Number of dogs actually enrolled vs excluded (BET073 absent)

Discrepancy: Notes sheet states high dose '10^9', whereas the CB101 note (full text) says 10^8 parasitised erythrocytes; low dose 10^4 agrees. Unresolved; dose label in the file should not be assumed correct. Overlap with DS01 (Mendeley 10.17632/zyhy8z69kt.1, CB101 data article): see overlaps. |

Abbreviation meanings can be recovered from the sibling deposit DS01 (stacked blocks with names such as Habitus, Heart rate, Appetite, Cell-free hemoglobin, Parasitemia) and from the DS01 supplementary table (habitus/appetite 1-4, temperature degC, pulse bpm). |

Dog 1 contributes only 1 baseline time point (clinical/haem/CRP/albumin) and nothing for cytokines, parasitaemia, lactate or iron. Dog 6 stops at day 4 (death). Column 4.1 is empty for dogs 4-6 and 5 and 7 are absent for cytokines. Missing = empty cell; no explicit missing code. Dog number is the join key across sheets. No omics annotation required. Values are stored with floating point artefacts (e.g. 38.200000000000003) from Excel. |

Explicit overlap check: all 1,503 numeric cells in DS01 'Raw data.xlsx' (stacked blocks of clinical variables, haematocrit, WCC, mature and band neutrophils, lymphocytes, monocytes, cell-free Hb, parasitaemia and 13 cytokines; plus the same Notes block with identical dose labels incl. 10^9) were found in DS02 as a multiset (100% of DS01 values; compared as numbers without dog IDs, so row-level alignment was not tested). DS02 is therefore a superset: it adds Hb, RCC, MCV, MCH, MCHC, RDW, eosinophils, basophils, platelets, NRBC, reticulocytes, CRP, albumin (about 688 additional cells) and provides explicit dog-number/group keys and descriptive column headers, but DS01 provides longer variable names. DS01 and DS02 must be counted as ONE set of 6 dogs (5 infected), not two independent datasets. Within DS02, all sheets repeat the same dogs (repeated measures, not independent samples). The DS01 supplementary docx contains group summary statistics derived from the same data. |

**Downloaded files (1; 0.03 MB):** `data/raw/DS02/B.Atkinson_Experimental Babesia study raw data.xlsx` (31,074 B, sha256 `a4a19a8d97e6…`)

**Suitable questions:** Descriptive temporal ordering of host response in experimental B. rossi infection: when CRP, neutrophils/bands, monocytes, cytokines (IL-6, IL-8, IL-10, MCP-1, IP-10, TNF-a, IFN-g) rise relative to parasitaemia, temperature, tachycardia, anaemia, lactate, MAP; effect of inoculum dose on onset and magnitude (n=2 vs 3); relation between parasitaemia/haemolysis markers (Hb, HCT, cell-free Hb) and inflammatory markers; post-treatment (days 5-8) inflammatory surge; per-analyte kinetic reference ranges for hypothesis generation or for planning sample size in natural-infection cohorts; use as a prior for assay behaviour (floor/ceiling values). Not suitable for prediction of outcome or severity classes, clustering into phenotypes, or any inference on natural tick-transmitted disease.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics and descriptive plots, not ML. Individual-dog trajectory plots with dose-group overlay; paired/within-dog change from baseline with exact or permutation tests; with n=5 infected dogs (3 vs 2) dose contrasts have very low power and a minimum achievable exact p-value is limited (for example 1/10 two-sided for 3 vs 2), so report effect sizes and ranges rather than significance. Linear mixed models with random intercept per dog (log-transformed cytokines/CRP, time as factor or spline) are feasible only for exploratory estimation; rank-based or area-under-curve summaries per dog with exact tests are more defensible. Treat censored/zero values with Tobit or detection-limit sensitivity analyses. If a multivariable analysis is attempted: penalised regression with leave-one-dog-out cross-validation only as a descriptive exercise; no train/test split can be meaningful with 5 infected dogs.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small-n/high-p: 5 infected dogs (3 vs 2), 1 uninfected donor with one baseline time point, versus about 50 variables x up to 11 time points (over 700 columns), so p >> n and any ML is overfit by construction. Repeated measures: 49 dog-time points are not independent; all splitting must be by dog, and leave-one-dog-out CV leaves 4 dogs for training. Treatment and selection confounding: all infected dogs treated with diminazene at pre-set endpoints; treatment time differs between dose groups (HD earlier), so dose, disease stage and treatment effect are confounded, and days 5-8 are post-treatment (including post-treatment cytokine surge per CB098); post-treatment values leak outcome/time information and should not be predictors for early severity. No outcome labels; death of dog 6 truncates series (informative missingness; the day-4 sample for dog 6 is terminal: Hb 70, ALB 11). No uninfected or sham-treated control group, so non-infection effects of sampling, anaesthesia (BP), fluid or handling cannot be separated. Dose label discrepancy: file states high dose 10^9 versus 10^8 in CB101 (unresolved). Males only, single donor inoculum, single source strain, laboratory beagles; cross-species and natural-disease comparability is uncertain (experimental IV inoculation, high-dose acute course, no co-infection, no breed or age variation). Cytokine panel is a canine Milliplex bead assay with no documented plate/batch layout, standard curves or detection limits; zeros and 10 mg/L CRP appear to be censored values. Duplicate DS01/DS02 must not be double counted in the review. Dog names appear in the file (Notes sheet), animal data only. Documentation of units and time-point meaning is incomplete and has to be taken from the articles. Never interpret any exploratory grouping of these dogs as subtypes.

**Unverified fields:** Exact meaning of the 4.1 time point (inferred from sparsity pattern); Unit of time (days post-infection inferred from article/Figshare description); Units of CRP, albumin, cytokines (pg/mL), parasitaemia (%), cell-free Hb and blood pressure; High-dose inoculum size (file says 10^9; CB101 note says 10^8); Treatment timing per dog and treatment drug (not in file; diminazene at about 96-108 h taken from CB101 note, not read in full text by this assessment); Cause and mode of death of dog 6 (inferred from truncation and CB101 note); Whether the Figshare record is formally linked to CB098 and/or CB101 (no reference field in the record; correspondence is by content only); Exact per-variable counts of isolated missing cells; Row-level (dog-by-dog) alignment between DS01 and DS02 (only multiset equality of numeric values tested); Interpretation of CRP value 10 and cytokine zeros as assay floor/imputed values; Whether 'Exp group 1' legend continues as high dose (truncated legend; inferred from dog numbers 4-6 labelled high dose); Europe PMC article licence check not applicable (no PMCID for CB098; dataset licence read from Figshare API)

<a id="s-cb517"></a>
### S-CB517 — Goddard et al. 2016 S1 and S2 Files (PDF tables): per-dog leukocyte differentials, SAA, CRP and seven serum cytokines, B. rossi dogs vs healthy controls (PLoS ONE pone.0150113.s001.pdf and .s002.pdf, PMC4783066)

**Why ranked 9:** Per-dog leukocyte differentials, SAA, CRP and 7 cytokines with survival for 112 dogs (97 B. rossi, 15 controls); its cytokines duplicate 112 of the S-CB050 dogs, so use it to add the acute phase proteins and differentials, not as replication.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Public download verified |
| Linked articles | [CB517](articles/CB517_2016_excessive-pro-inflammatory-serum-cytokine-concentrations.md) |
| Correspondence check | Matches the article note CB517 on every checked point. (1) Counts: both PDFs hold the same 112 IDs: Case1 to Case97 (97 infected, no gaps in numbering) and Control1 to Control15. This equals the note's 97 infected dogs and 15 controls. (2) Outcome: 85 Survived and 12 Died, equal to the note's 85 survivors and 12 non-survivors; the Outcome label for each of the 97 cases is the same in S1 and S2. The 12 deaths are Case13, 21, 34, 37, 55, 58, 59, 61, 65, 81, 84, 85. (3) Content: S1 is leukocyte data and S2 is APP and cytokine data, as the note lists. S2 has the seven cytokines the note names (IL-2, IL-6, IL-8, IL-10, IL-18, GM-CSF, MCP-1) plus SAA and CRP. (4) Medians I recomputed from the rows reproduce the note's figures: IL-8 1066.3 (infected) vs 3316.7 (controls) pg/mL; IL-10 620.3 vs 44.6; MCP-1 544.2 vs 211.7. Direction agrees with the note for IL-6 (median 643.9 in non-survivors vs 84.1 in survivors) and MCP-1 (921.7 vs 527.6); I did not recompute P values. SAA and CRP are raised in infected dogs (medians 933.4 vs 14.1 mg/L; 111.3 vs 2.6 mg/L), as the note says. WBC, neutrophil, lymphocyte and eosinophil medians are lower in infected dogs than controls (6.29 vs 10.57; 4.25 vs 5.80; 1.33 vs 2.08; 0.00 vs 0.73), as the note says; monocytes are about equal (0.53 vs 0.54). Discrepancies: none in counts. The note's days-sick data (84 dogs), sex and the other clinical findings are not in the files, so those parts of the note cannot be checked here. |
| Repository / accession | Europe PMC / PMC supplementary files (PLOS ONE Supporting Information, S1 File and S2 File) / PMCID PMC4783066; article DOI 10.1371/journal.pone.0150113; PMID 26953797; files pone.0150113.s001.pdf and pone.0150113.s002.pdf |
| Landing page | https://europepmc.org/article/PMC/PMC4783066 |
| Licence / reuse terms | article licence (Europe PMC open-access record); not separately specified |
| Species | Domestic dog (Canis lupus familiaris), client-owned, naturally infected with Babesia rossi (PCR and reverse line blot per the article note); South Africa (Onderstepoort Veterinary Academic Hospital) |
| Design | Case-control, cross-sectional. One admission blood sample per dog, taken before treatment (per the article note): 97 infected dogs and 15 healthy controls. Outcome is survival to discharge vs death or euthanasia (infected dogs only). Cohort CH03 (Oct 2011 to Apr 2013). The files are two published PDF tables with the same dogs; they hold no day-sick, sex, age or treatment data. |
| Granularity | individual dog/sample-level |
| Raw vs processed | Individual-level values printed in two supplementary PDF tables (3 pages each). They are processed values, not instrument exports: the cytokine values are multiplex bead concentrations (pg/mL), and values below the detection limit were set to the minimum detectable concentration (per the article note). Repeated floor values show this: IL-2 3.4, IL-6 3.6, IL-10 8.4, IL-18 5.7, GM-CSF 9.1. The ceiling value 50000.0 appears in 4 cells (Case80: IL-2, IL-6, IL-18, GM-CSF). The zip also holds 10 image files (JPG and GIF versions of Table 1 and Figures 1 to 4 of the article, judged from the names only), which carry no new data. Only the two PDFs are tables. |
| Independent animals | 112 — Counted from the dump: 112 unique IDs in each PDF, and the two ID sets are identical. S1: 97 Case rows and 15 Control rows. S2: 97 Case rows and 15 Control rows. 97 infected + 15 controls = 112 dogs, equal to the article note. One row per dog per file, so no repeated measures. The same 112 dogs appear in both files, so the rows of S1 and S2 must be joined by ID and not stacked. |
| Samples | 112 |
| Groups and controls | Infected dogs n=97: survivors 85, non-survivors 12 (died or euthanised for poor prognosis, per the article note). Healthy controls n=15, with no Outcome value (the control rows have one column fewer). Control selection is described only in the article (healthy dogs for ovariohysterectomy, castration or blood donation, per the note); the files do not say so. No age, weight, sex, breed or days-sick columns, so the group sex imbalance reported in the article (P = 0.02) cannot be checked or adjusted for here. |
| Time points | Single time point per dog (admission, before treatment per the article note; the files do not state the timing). No longitudinal data. |
| Features | S1 (leukocytes, 5 values per dog): WBC, NEUT, LYMPH, MONO, EOS, all x10^9/L. S2 (11 columns per dog in infected rows): Case no, Outcome, SAA (mg/L), CRP (mg/L), IL-2, IL-6, IL-8, IL-10, IL-18, GM-CSF, MCP-1 (all pg/mL). In total 14 measured variables plus ID and outcome. The units are in the column headers. Absent: band neutrophils, basophils, platelets, haematocrit, biochemistry, parasitaemia, lactate, clinical signs, days sick, age, sex, weight, treatment. The article's Table 1 and clinical abnormality data are only in images. |
| Outcomes | Binary survival (Survived 85 vs Died 12) for infected dogs, and infected vs control. No severity score or complicated/uncomplicated label. Cause of death is not in the files. |
| Data dictionary | Minimal. Column headers carry names and units. There is no legend for the Outcome values, no definition of NEUT (segmented only, or segmented plus band), and no note on floor or ceiling handling in the files (the floor rule is in the article only). The only Outcome codes are Survived and Died, and controls have none. No separate dictionary file. |
| Missingness | No blank cells: all 97 infected and 15 control rows are complete in both files (S1 rows have 7 or 6 values, S2 rows 11 or 10, as expected). Controls have no Outcome by design. Many cytokine values are at the assay floor, so they are left-censored or imputed: IL-2 3.4 in 30 of 112 dogs (27 infected, 3 controls); GM-CSF 9.1 in 28 of 112; IL-6 3.6 in 11; IL-10 8.4 in 5 (all controls); IL-18 5.7 in 4. Four values in Case80 are at the 50000.0 ceiling (IL-2, IL-6, IL-18, GM-CSF), and I assume they are right-censored. Eosinophils are 0.00 in 60 of 97 infected dogs and in none of the controls. Possible entry errors in S1: in 17 infected dogs NEUT + LYMPH + MONO + EOS is more than 25 percent below WBC (for example Case43 WBC 22.06 with NEUT 4.25; Case35 WBC 16.10 with NEUT 4.70; Case67 NEUT 0.14 with WBC 5.38). This may be unlisted band neutrophils or typing errors; it cannot be settled from the files. |
| Technical requirements | Needs PDF table extraction (for example pdfplumber or camelot) and a join of S1 and S2 on the ID. The PDF text comes out clean as one cell per line, and I parsed all 224 rows without gaps, so the parse is easy but must be scripted and checked. Values are decimal points and fit in memory. Treat 3.4, 3.6, 8.4, 5.7 and 9.1 as left-censored and 50000.0 as right-censored. Use rank-based or censoring-aware methods. Nothing else is needed. |
| Files | PMC4783066 supplementaryFiles zip with 12 members. Two data PDFs: pone.0150113.s001.pdf (43,733 bytes, 3 pages, leukocyte data for 97 cases and 15 controls) and pone.0150113.s002.pdf (53,684 bytes, 3 pages, SAA, CRP and cytokines for the same 112 dogs). Ten image members (pone.0150113.g001 to g004 and t001, each as JPG and GIF, 7.9 to 86.5 KB) are renderings of article figures and Table 1; I judged them from file names only and did not open them. Member sizes sum to 405,413 bytes; the zip file size and SHA-256 are not in the inspection summary. |
| Overlaps | Partly duplicated by S-CB050 (Celliers 2020 supplement, 161 dogs). S-CB050 says its cohort-1 dogs (cases 1 to 97 infected, controls 201 to 215) are these CH03 dogs, with the same seven cytokines (IL-2, IL-6, IL-8, IL-10, IL-18, GM-CSF, MCP-1) for 97 infected + 15 controls, so S-CB517 duplicates the cytokine data of 112 of S-CB050's 161 dogs, probably value for value (S-CB050 uses the same floors, for example IL-2 3.4, and a ceiling of 50001.0 for Case80 against 50000.0 here). The 112 dogs are not independent of S-CB050 and the two must not be pooled. I did not compare values between the two files, so the match is unverified. What S-CB517 adds: SAA and CRP (acute phase proteins, absent from S-CB050), and full leukocyte differentials for the same dogs (LYMPH, MONO, EOS and total NEUT; S-CB050 has only WBC, segmented neutrophils and band neutrophils, and no lymphocytes, monocytes or eosinophils). What S-CB050 has and S-CB517 lacks: age, weight, sex, MPXI, band neutrophils, and cohort 2 (44 infected, 5 controls, no cytokines). S-CB050's own assessment lists 97 infected dogs in cohort 1 against 96 reported; this file also has 97 infected dogs (matching the CB517 note), which suggests the 96 is a miscount in the S-CB050 article note. Within this dataset, no repeated dogs. |

**Downloaded files (1; 0.33 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB517/PMC4783066_supplementaryFiles.zip` (333,608 B, sha256 `9098a57a707b…`)

**Suitable questions:** (1) Reproduce the article's main comparisons at dog level: IL-10, MCP-1 and IL-8 in infected dogs vs controls; IL-6 and MCP-1 in non-survivors vs survivors; SAA and CRP in infected dogs vs controls and by outcome. (2) Test how SAA, CRP, leukocyte counts and cytokines relate to each other, by outcome (for example IL-8 and IL-10 against WBC, neutrophils, monocytes, as the article reports weak correlations). (3) Use the SAA and CRP columns and the lymphocyte, monocyte and eosinophil counts as the added layer for the CH03 dogs when linked by ID to other CH03 datasets. (4) Describe eosinopenia (60 of 97 infected dogs at 0.00) and the lymphocyte and neutrophil counts against outcome. Not suitable: anything needing sex, age, days sick, parasitaemia, biochemistry or clinical signs; prediction models with only 12 deaths; time courses; causal claims; treating S-CB517 as a cohort independent of S-CB050.

**Recommended methods:** Use ordinary statistics. Mann-Whitney or exact permutation tests with Holm or BH correction for infected vs control and survivor vs non-survivor; effect sizes with bootstrap confidence intervals; Spearman correlations with correction, reported within group only (12 non-survivors is too few for stable correlations). Treat floor values as left-censored and 50000.0 as right-censored (Tobit or rank methods, or dichotomise IL-2, GM-CSF and IL-6). Log-transform cytokines, SAA and CRP. For prognosis, if tried at all, use Firth or ridge logistic regression with 1 to 2 pre-specified markers (IL-6, MCP-1) and bootstrap optimism-corrected AUC; no train/test split is feasible with 12 events. Join S1 and S2 by ID first. Do not cluster or call clusters subtypes. If S-CB050 is used too, keep one copy of the cytokine data and treat both as the same dogs.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small for its features: 12 deaths, so outcome analyses are underpowered and prediction models would overfit; no external validation set. Minimal covariates: no sex, age, weight, breed or days sick, although the article reports a sex imbalance between groups and a days-sick effect on IL-2, IL-6, IL-18 and GM-CSF, so confounding cannot be handled from these files. Control selection and matching are not in the files. Outcome includes euthanasia for poor prognosis (per the article note), so it partly reflects clinician or owner decisions. Sampling time relative to treatment is not stated in the files. Censoring: many cytokine values are assay-floor values (IL-2 and GM-CSF in about a quarter of dogs) and 4 cells in Case80 are at a 50000.0 ceiling; this distorts distributions and correlations. Possible entry errors in the leukocyte table (17 infected rows with differentials far below WBC; Case67 NEUT 0.14). Differentials are incomplete (no bands, no basophils), so the sum of parts does not equal WBC. Serum cytokines may not reflect bioactive or local levels, and TNF and IFN-gamma were not measured (per the note). The multiplex panel is not comparable in absolute pg/mL with other panels or species. Not independent of S-CB050. Data are PDFs: they need scripted extraction and checking, and are a published table, not an instrument export. Associational only.

**Unverified fields:** Whether the CB517 dogs and values are identical, case for case, to cases 1 to 97 and controls 201 to 215 in S-CB050 (inferred from S-CB050's assessment and the shared panel; values not compared); Whether the 97 vs 96 infected dogs in cohort 1 is a miscount in the S-CB050 note (inferred only); Timing of sampling relative to treatment and illness onset (taken from the article note, not stated in the files); Whether NEUT is segmented neutrophils only or includes bands; Cause of the large gaps between WBC and the sum of the differentials in 17 infected dogs (bands, other cells or entry errors); Whether 50000.0 is an upper assay limit or an imputed value, and the exact meaning of the floor values (floor rule taken from the article note); Control selection and sex/age match (not in files); P values and the survivor/non-survivor tests (directions and medians checked, tests not recomputed); Content of the 10 image members (not opened; judged from file names only); Zip file size and SHA-256 (not in the inspection summary); Whether the PDF tables match the article's days-sick subgroups (days sick not in files)

### DS04 — Liver histopathology score sheets, immunohistochemistry counts, haematology and serum biochemistry for 10 fatal Babesia rossi-infected dogs and 4 healthy controls (University of Pretoria Research Data, Figshare; thesis tables)

**Why ranked 10:** Fatal B. rossi liver histopathology/IHC score sheets with haematology/biochemistry (Onderstepoort tissue bank, CH04).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Public download verified |
| Linked articles | [CB147](articles/CB147_2026_liver-histopathology-naturally-acquired-babesia-rossi.md) |
| Correspondence check | Corresponds to CB147 (Horrell, Leisewitz, Clift 2026, liver histopathology in B. rossi dogs); does NOT correspond to CB111 (lung). Evidence: (1) Group sizes match: Stats Tables.xlsx has 14 case IDs (4 controls: 2, 4, 9, 11; 10 infected: 20, 50, 54, 59, 61, 88, 91, 106, 110, 117), and thesis Tables 2-13 state n=4 vs n=10, as in CB147. (2) Tissue is liver in every file (per-case sheets are 'Liver histopathology' templates with zones, Kupffer cells, space of Disse, bile, haemosiderin; the spaces of Disse, bile, Kupffer cell and extramedullary haematopoiesis items match the CB147 abstract). (3) Numerical matches with the CB147 note: ALT median 86.5 vs 21.4 U/L (p=0.002) appears in thesis Table 3 and is reproducible from stats for haematology.xlsx; urea control median 3.1 mmol/L, p=0.048 matches; MAC387 non-zonal infected 260.5 vs control 27.0 = 9.6-fold (thesis Table 13 prints '9.-fold') matches the CB147 '9.6-fold'; markers MAC387/Iba-1/CD204 (CB147 mentions Iba-1 antibody). (4) Infected urea median differs: CB147 note gives 28.3, thesis Table 3 gives 20.7. Recomputing from the xlsx shows 28.3 is the median of the 7 non-zero values and 20.7 is the median when 3 zero placeholders (missing) are included, so the thesis table contains a missing-as-zero error that the article seems to have corrected. This also shows that the dataset is the thesis version, not necessarily identical to the published tables. (5) The set shares the same dogs as the group's other organ studies (CB147 note says same dogs as spleen, lung and bone marrow), but no lung data are present, so CB111 is not supported. Not verified: CB147 Supplementary S1 / Table_1/2 DOCX were not opened, so a file-by-file match with the published supplement is unconfirmed; the dissertation (hdl 2263/98495) itself was not retrieved. |
| Repository / accession | University of Pretoria Research Data (Figshare); related UP repository item hdl.handle.net/2263/98495 / 10.25403/UPresearchdata.25130762.v1 |
| Landing page | https://doi.org/10.25403/UPresearchdata.25130762.v1 |
| Licence / reuse terms | CC BY 4.0, taken from the known Figshare record metadata supplied for this task; not re-read on the live record and not checked on the CB147 Europe PMC record. |
| Species | Domestic dog (Canis lupus familiaris), naturally infected Babesia rossi (fatal cases, South Africa) plus healthy control dogs; many breeds (Great Dane, Boerboel, GSD, Rottweiler, Boxer, Cocker spaniel, Pitbull, JRT etc.) |
| Design | Post-mortem liver tissue case-control series (10 fatal B. rossi dogs vs 4 healthy controls), single terminal sampling per dog. Semi-quantitative histopathology (per-zone ordinal 0-3 scores, ~70 items on a sheet template for periportal, midzonal, centrilobular zones), IHC cell counts per 400x high-power field (3 fields per zone per dog, markers CD3, CD20, CD204, Iba-1, MAC387, MUM-1), special stains (MSB, Perls' 'Pearls', LFB), admission haematology and serum biochemistry. Each liver was scored 2-4 times (sheets .1, .2, .3 dated 23 Jan to 4 Apr 2022); these are repeat scoring sessions, not clinical time points. All infected dogs died or were euthanised, so there is no outcome contrast within infected dogs. |
| Granularity | individual dog/sample-level, summary statistics only |
| Raw vs processed | Mixed. Raw-ish individual-level: per-HPF IHC counts (Stats Tables 'IHC', 'Special Stains' sheets, 42 field rows x 18 variables), per-dog haematology/biochemistry rows, per-case histopathology score sheets (observer ordinal scores, so observer-derived rather than instrument data). Processed: per-dog mean-of-3-HPF table ('Sheet1'), 'Total Phenotypes' sheet, and the thesis docx (12 summary tables of medians/SD/ranges/p-values, which is summary statistics only). No images, no raw instrument output. |
| Independent animals | 14 — Counted from unique case IDs in Stats Tables.xlsx (IHC, Special Stains and Score Sheet sheets): 4 controls (IDs 2, 4, 9, 11) and 10 infected (20, 50, 54, 59, 61, 88, 91, 106, 110, 117). The per-case files use 16 ID prefixes (2, 2A, 4, 4A, 9A, 11A plus 10 infected) that collapse to 14 only after merging 2/2A and 4/4A. Haematology/biochemistry sheets have 4 + 10 rows with no ID column, so 14 there is by row count only. Identity of control dogs is not fully verifiable: sheets 4A.1/4A.2 give 'Dexter, 3 y, JRT, 7 kg' while 4.1/4A.3 give 'Pitbull, 7 months, 12 kg'; 9A.3 gives 'bull terrier 23 kg' vs 9A.1/9A.2 'Africanis 13 kg'; and infected case 88 ('Pitbull, M, 7 m, 12 kg') has the same signalment as control 4. |
| Samples | 14 |
| Groups and controls | Control (n=4, healthy dogs, younger 6 months to 3 y, lighter 6.5-23 kg, different breeds; flagged 0) vs infected (n=10, all B. rossi, all fatal, flagged 1). Infected dogs' signalment (free text): ages 9 months to 12 y; 4 died before or on arrival (cases 20, 50, 88 DOA, 117 on admission), 6 died after treatment (106, 110, 54, 59, 61, 91; treatments listed: berenil/diminazene, blood transfusion, fluids, O2, glucose, clopamon, cerenia, prednisolone, lasix, voluven). Mucous membrane colour recorded per dog (icteric, pale, cyanotic). Controls were not measured for bilirubin or glucose. Parasite species/PCR: the RT-qPCR row on every sheet is blank; confirmation is only per the article. No survivors and no uncomplicated babesiosis group. |
| Time points | No longitudinal sampling. One post-mortem liver per dog; haematology/biochemistry presumably at admission (timing relative to death not stated in files). Score sheets are repeat scorings of the same specimen (controls: 2 has 3 sessions, 4 has 4, 9 has 3, 11 has 3 listed in Score Sheet; infected: 2 sessions each) carried out 23 Jan to 4 Apr 2022; controls were mostly scored Jan to early Mar 2022 and infected mostly Mar to Apr 2022. |
| Features | Histopathology sheet: autolysis, congestion, haemorrhage, and ~70 ordinal 0-3 items across periportal, midzonal and centrilobular zones (bile stasis/proliferation, hepatocyte necrosis, hydropic change, fatty change, vesicular nuclei, turnover, anisokaryosis, pigments lipofuscin/haemosiderin/bile, vessel leukostasis/endothelium, lymphatic distension, fibrin, extramedullary haematopoiesis, space of Disse oedema, cellular infiltrates, Kupffer cell activation/haemosiderin/bile/injury, intracellular parasites); a total score. IHC: 6 markers x 3 zones (CV/MZ/PT) x 3 HPF per dog = 18 count variables (counts per 400x HPF, 756 cells). Special stains: MSB, Perls, LFB x 3 zones (ordinal or counts). Haematology (9 variables): RBC (x10^12/L), Hct (L/L), reticulocytes %, platelets, WCC, neutrophils, bands, lymphocytes, monocytes (x10^9/L). Biochemistry (10 variables): TSP, albumin, globulin (g/L), ALT, ALP (U/L), total bilirubin (umol/L), glucose, urea (mmol/L), bile acids pre/post (umol/L). Signalment as free text (breed, age, sex, weight, outcome, treatments). |
| Outcomes | Only 'fatal' for all 10 infected dogs (plus free-text died before Rx / after Rx / DOA / on admission in signalment, partially a proxy for survival time and treatment exposure); control vs infected group flag (0/1); icterus colour description. No severity grade or survival time. |
| Data dictionary | Partial / informal. Units are given in column headers for haematology/biochemistry and reference ranges are listed; IHC unit (cells per 400x HPF) and column codes (CV/MZ/PT, MAC, MUM, MSB, Pearls, LFB, Control/Case 0/1) are only explained in the thesis docx captions. Absent: no README or codebook; score scale meaning (0-3) and the 'out of 126' denominator are unexplained (about 70 scored items sum to e.g. 71); the second unlabelled score column present in 9 sheets is undocumented; haem/biochem rows have no case ID; zero is used as a missing-value code (bilirubin/glucose in controls, urea and bile acids in 3/10 infected, post-prandial bile acids absent for infected), with 'x', '<1' and '>148' as text codes. |
| Missingness | Low for IHC (13 of 756 cells blank, 1.7%, e.g. missing Iba-1 and CD3 fields). Histopathology sheets: about 70 scored items per populated sheet with 1-3 blanks in a few sheets; non-numeric fields (congestion/haemorrhage/zone distribution, RT-qPCR) are blank or text. Two of 33 listed per-case sheets are blank templates (88.1 and the duplicate 50.2 (1)), and the third scoring of control 11 (score 30) is listed in the Score Sheet without a corresponding file. Haem/biochem: controls have no bilirubin/glucose (4/4); infected have urea 0 (3/10) and bile acids pre 0 (3/10) as placeholders, bile acids post 10/10 missing, one globulin 'x', reticulocyte zeros ambiguous (true zero or not done); so zero cannot be taken at face value. |
| Technical requirements | Excel and Word reading only (stdlib or openpyxl/pandas; python-docx for tables). Per-case sheets are layout templates (merged cells; scores in column 15 and sometimes 17), so a custom parser mapping row labels (zone/cell/feature) to scores is needed; free-text signalment needs manual coding. No sequencing, annotation or proprietary software required. Whole set 37 files, 4.14 MB (33 per-case xlsx about 120-127 KB each, Stats Tables.xlsx 27 KB, 2 haematology xlsx 17-18 KB, thesis docx 58 KB). |
| Files | 37 files, 4,141,423 bytes: 33 per-case histopathology score sheets (.xlsx; 31 populated, 2 blank; named S-number.session + date + total, e.g. '106.1 21.03.2022 - 71-126.xlsx'); 'Stats Tables.xlsx' (5 sheets: IHC, Sheet1 per-dog means, Special Stains, Score Sheet, Total Phenotypes); 'stats for haematology.xlsx' and '(1)' duplicate without summary rows (sheets Haematology, Biochemistries); 'all tables from thesis.docx' (12 tables: haematology, biochemistry, IHC control and per-case/marker summaries). |
| Overlaps | Same dogs as the group's spleen, lung (CB111 likely) and bone marrow studies, per the CB147 note, but cases in this dataset cannot be linked to those studies' data from these files. Near-duplicate files: 50.2 has a blank '(1)' copy; 'stats for haematology (1).xlsx' duplicates the other without summary rows. Each dog has 2-4 repeat scoring records and 3 HPF per zone, so rows are not independent. Control and case 88 share an identical signalment (possible template carry-over). |

**Downloaded files (37; 4.14 MB):** `data/raw/DS04/2.1 05.03.2022 - 15-126.xlsx` (127,335 B, sha256 `aef716d291c9…`); `data/raw/DS04/2A.1 23.01.2022 - 20-126.xlsx` (119,425 B, sha256 `2ac8cd39d563…`); `data/raw/DS04/2A.2 25.01.2022 - 19-126.xlsx` (121,024 B, sha256 `e331393d4785…`); `data/raw/DS04/4.1 05.03.2022 - 14-126.xlsx` (121,615 B, sha256 `17e736a5f94d…`); `data/raw/DS04/4A.1 23.01 - 15-126.xlsx` (120,583 B, sha256 `752531b753c5…`); `data/raw/DS04/4A.2 25.01.2022 - 18-126.xlsx` (120,832 B, sha256 `e6451d590ead…`); `data/raw/DS04/4A.3 03.02.2022- 15-126.xlsx` (120,560 B, sha256 `1391ab3039b0…`); `data/raw/DS04/9A.1 23.01.2022 - 20-126.xlsx` (120,581 B, sha256 `2cde0ce520ca…`); … 29 more in the manifest

**Suitable questions:** Descriptive hypothesis-generating questions on fatal B. rossi liver pathology: zone-specific (centrilobular) hepatocyte necrosis/hydropic change vs haemolysis markers (RBC, Hct, bilirubin) and macrophage infiltration (MAC387, CD204, Iba-1) in 10 vs 4 dogs; association of macrophage burden with haemosiderin/bile load and with ALT; zonal distribution of leucocyte phenotypes (portal vs midzonal vs centrilobular); intra-observer reproducibility of semi-quantitative scores across repeat sessions (a methods question). Useful as tissue-level evidence for an inflammation/haemolysis/hypoxia model of complicated disease. Not suitable for prognostic modelling or subtype discovery (no outcome variation, n=14).

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics, not ML. Exact/permutation Mann-Whitney or Wilcoxon tests with effect sizes and bootstrap CIs at dog level; for IHC use dog-level means or a mixed model (negative binomial or log-count with dog as random effect, zone as fixed effect); ordinal scores via proportional-odds or exact tests after averaging or modelling repeat sessions (weighted kappa/ICC for reproducibility); Spearman correlation among markers and clinical variables with multiplicity control; penalised regression with nested leave-one-dog-out CV only as exploratory with explicit warning. Treat zeros as missing before any analysis, recode censored values ('<1', '>148') properly, and do not report or call clusters subtypes.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n/high p: 14 dogs against ~70 histology items, 18 IHC variables, 19 clinical variables; any multivariate model would be hopelessly overfit, and no external validation set exists. Selection/treatment confounding: infected group is fatal-only, with variable pre-death treatment (diminazene, transfusion, fluids), time to death, age 9 months to 12 y and body weight 3-45 kg; controls are younger, lighter, differently bred and probably from different handling, so group effects are confounded with age/breed/source and post-mortem interval/autolysis. Batch/observer effects: controls were scored mostly in Jan-early Mar 2022 and infected in Mar-Apr 2022, with unblinded semi-quantitative scoring and an unexplained second score column; IHC run batches unknown. Outcome leakage: group label is perfectly defined by infection status and many histology items define the lesion; haem/biochem are largely disease-defining; no severity labels, so ML 'classification' would be trivial. Repeated measures: 31 populated sheets map to 14 dogs and 42 fields map to 14 dogs, so splits must be by dog (leave-one-dog-out); haem/biochem cannot be reliably linked to case IDs (row order only). Data quality: zero-as-missing coding produced wrong medians in the thesis (urea 20.7 vs 28.3, bile acids), summary text vs data discrepancies (WCC max 105.6 in text vs 103.55 in data; band range), ID/signalment inconsistencies among control sheets and case 88. Cross-species comparability: canine antibodies and scoring scheme are non-standard; comparison with human malaria/sepsis liver pathology would be qualitative only. Dataset is thesis-level, and not identical to the published supplement.

**Unverified fields:** licence (CC BY 4.0 from supplied metadata, not re-read on the live Figshare record or the CB147 Europe PMC record); link between haematology/biochemistry rows and case IDs (no ID column; row order assumed); identity of control dogs 4, 9 (conflicting signalment across sheets); timing of blood sampling relative to death/admission; meaning of the second unlabelled score column and of the 'out of 126' denominator; whether the number of liver sections per dog is more than one (n_samples counted as 14 liver specimens); file-by-file match with the CB147 published supplement (Supplementary S1/Table_1/Table_2 not opened); correspondence to the thesis document itself (hdl.handle.net/2263/98495 not retrieved); PCR/RLB confirmation of mono-infection (RT-qPCR fields blank in files); mapping of Special Stains sheet values to scale (MSB/Pearls/LFB ordinal vs counts); sex/age of controls beyond free text

**Downloaded files (1; 1.25 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB147/PMC13283813_supplementaryFiles.zip` (1,246,778 B, sha256 `f4852514d6b0…`)

**Suitable questions:** Descriptive tissue immunophenotype of fatal B. rossi hepatic injury: is the hepatic leukocyte response monocyte-macrophage dominated (MAC387, CD204, Iba-1) with a zonal gradient (midzonal and portal vs centrilobular)? Use for mechanistic evidence tables on liver as a secondary target (haemolysis/hypoxia plus activated macrophage response), for extracting reported effect sizes (medians, fold changes, P-values) and for building a variable list for the liver histopathology score (via the rubric). With DS04 (if confirmed) one could relate per-dog histology scores to the IHC counts across the same 10 dogs, descriptively. Not suitable for diagnosis, prognosis, severity classification or biomarker discovery, because there is no outcome variation and no clinical covariates.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics, not ML. For infected vs control: exact or permutation tests (Mann-Whitney / Wilcoxon rank-sum with exact P, n=10 vs 4, minimum attainable P limited), effect sizes with bootstrap CIs, treating the dog as the unit; for zone comparisons within infected dogs, paired non-parametric tests (Friedman / Wilcoxon signed rank) or a mixed model with dog as random effect and zone as fixed effect (counts on a log or negative-binomial scale; only 10 dogs, so keep models simple), with multiplicity control across 6 markers x 3 zones. Because control values are group-level only, a per-dog control analysis cannot be re-run: reuse the published control medians as reference ranges and report the original P-values rather than recomputing. Correlation among the markers (e.g. Spearman) is descriptive only. Do not fit predictive ML, do not cluster the 10 fatal cases into "subtypes".

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n / high p: 10 infected dogs, 4 controls, 18 correlated marker-zone variables, which gives very low power and makes any multivariate model unstable. Control group is only 4 dogs, younger (median 15.5 months), lighter, different breeds and not matched; controls are available only as group medians, so no per-dog variance or individual-level control analysis is possible. Selection and survivorship bias: fatal cases only (end-stage snapshot), no survivors or uncomplicated disease, so severity or prognosis questions cannot be tested and outcome leakage is moot only because there is no outcome variation. Treatment confounding: infected dogs received treatment (e.g. diminazene, blood, oxygen, per the DS04 signalment notes) before death; duration of illness and treatment are not in this bundle. Post-mortem and autolysis effects (sampling within 1 h of death, fixation up to 5 days; autolysis is scored in the rubric but not provided per dog). Per-case values are summaries of unknown aggregation; the control medians are repeated on every row, so rows are not independent observations, and the repeated P-values must not be treated as per-dog evidence. Manual counting by hand on photomicrographs, so observer variability and no inter-rater data. Zones within a dog are repeated measures: any model must split and resample at the dog level, never by row. MAC387 also labels neutrophils (rare here, 2/10 cases noted). Cross-species comparability: human malaria and sepsis liver pathology are compared only qualitatively by the authors, antibody cross-reactivity in dog is from prior studies and manufacturers, so counts are not directly comparable across species. Bundle contains no raw per-field counts, no clinical pathology (ALT, urea, haematology) per dog and no per-dog histology score data, so Table_1 cannot be used for analysis. The article lists only 'article/supplement, further inquiries to corresponding author' for data availability.

**Unverified fields:** n_animals: control n=4 taken from captions and article only (controls not itemised in the data); Aggregation rule behind per-case infected values (number of HPF, mean vs median) is inferred from decimal thirds; Statistical test behind the repeated P-values is not stated in the supplement; Licence applies to the article per the Europe PMC record; no supplement-specific licence was shown; DS04 overlap with CB147 (same 10 dogs) is inferred from case numbers 106 and 110 and the matching liver histopathology score sheets; not confirmed; Content of the figure files (JPG/GIF) was not examined beyond the article captions; Bold formatting (significance flags) and any shading or images embedded in Table_1.DOCX were not inspected; Clinical pathology values per dog, treatment details and time to death are not in the bundle and are not verified from the article; Exact correspondence of Table 3 article cohort medians for CD3 (12/45.5/5.0 in the article's Table 3 vs 9/47/5 as the supplement's totals): apparent difference between article Table 3 and the supplement's total medians for CD3 portal and midzonal was seen and not reconciled

Row order differs (Table 1A has the A, B, C, Control order and Table 2A has Control first), and no shared ID links individual dogs across assays. Sample count in the abstract (22 + 12) matches. No dog IDs, so cross-study overlap cannot be checked. |

**Downloaded files (1; 2.75 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB071/PMC8484968_supplementaryFiles.zip` (2,746,682 B, sha256 `e0fe2de20b06…`)

**Suitable questions:** (1) Reproduce or re-test group differences in urinary amino acids, acylcarnitines and energy metabolites across Control, A, B and C, with effect sizes and CIs, ordered by kidney-function severity (trend tests). (2) Describe the targeted versus untargeted agreement for the metabolites measured by both (for example Arg, Gln, His, Phe, Pro, Trp, Thr, Gly, Glu). (3) Provide effect-size priors for renal and metabolic involvement in babesial AKI and proteinuria, for a scoping review of metabolomic evidence in canine babesiosis. (4) Pathway-level hypotheses such as aminoacyl-tRNA biosynthesis, histidine metabolism, urea cycle and carnitine shuttle, as hypothesis-generating only. Not suitable: prognosis or outcome prediction (no outcome labels), diagnostic accuracy for babesiosis versus other AKI (no comparator), infection-specific biomarker claims, and repeated-measures or longitudinal questions.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are more defensible than ML. With n = 34 (6/10/6/12) and 3207 features (p much larger than n), use per-feature linear models or one-way ANOVA/Kruskal-Wallis on log-transformed intensities with FDR control. Add ordered-group trend tests (Jonckheere-Terpstra) for the Control to A to B to C gradient, and exact or permutation tests for planned contrasts. Use censored or left-censored models, or sensitivity analyses, for the NAs. For multivariable or exploratory work use penalised regression (elastic net, sparse PLS-DA) with nested cross-validation and a permutation null, reporting stability of selection, and treat any clustering as exploratory (clusters are not subtypes). Mixed models are not needed because there is one sample per dog. Use the stated ANOVA results only as published and re-run them from Table 1A and Table 2A for verification.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n and high p: the smallest groups have 6 dogs, so power is low and ML would overfit, with nested CV on 34 samples being unstable. Batch effects: the sample suffixes _B (infected) and _Z (controls) may show the groups were processed or collected separately, which would confound the group effect with run order and batch (confounding not verifiable, and run order and QC samples are not provided). Group definition leakage: groups are defined by creatinine and UPC, so any model using creatinine, or metabolites such as urinary creatinine, creatine or amino acid excretion tied to renal function, will recover the label by construction. Table 1A includes creatinine (peak 1) and Table 2 includes a creatinine-related column in correlations, so do not use them as predictors of group. No per-dog UPC or creatinine values, so the A/B/C labels cannot be checked and continuous modelling is impossible. Selection confounding: dogs admitted to a referral clinic, a single centre, unreported recruitment period, no age, sex, breed, diet, hydration or urine-concentration data, and urine metabolites are not normalised in the files, so dilution can confound group differences. Control dogs were healthy, with no non-babesial AKI or sick-control comparator, so the findings may reflect generic AKI or inflammation, not Babesia. No external validation set. All urine was before treatment, so there is no post-treatment leakage but also no outcome. Animal-level splitting: one sample per dog, so a simple sample-level split is also animal-level, provided the assumption holds (dog IDs are not given). Cross-species: dog urine, with human CKD/AKI comparison only at the pathway level, no direct comparability, and Biocrates and LC-MS platforms differ. Only 75 untargeted features are standard-confirmed, and the others are putative annotations. The same samples feed the untargeted and targeted tables, so they are not independent confirmations. The ANOVA tables were selected after testing, so do not use them as independent evidence.

**Unverified fields:** One sample per dog (no dog IDs in files); Group definitions A/B/C (creatinine and UPC cut-offs; only from article note, no per-dog values); Sample overlap between Table 1 untargeted and Table 2 targeted assays (no linking IDs); Overlap of dogs with other publications; Targeted platform identity (Biocrates p400 HR taken from the article note, not stated in the files); Units of Table 2A concentrations and normalisation of Table 1A intensities; Batch or run-order structure and QC samples; Meaning of _B and _Z sample suffixes; n used for correlations in Table 3 (not stated; UPC values not given); Reason for NA values (below LOD or missing); Licence for the supplement itself (article licence CC BY applied); Accession of raw MS data (none found; not checked beyond the article note)

**Downloaded files (1; 2.03 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB091/PMC8835742_supplementaryFiles.zip` (2,029,912 B, sha256 `7dc017c3db47…`)

**Suitable questions:** Which serum metabolites and pathways differ between B. canis-infected and healthy dogs (hypothesis-generating; amino acid, glutathione/sulphur amino acid, kynurenine/inosine, lysophospholipid and glycerolipid signal)? Effect-size estimation and replication of reported differences (e.g. Inosine, L-kynurenine, LPC(22:6), DG(36:2)) as a reference for later canine studies, and a cross-species comparison of metabolite classes against human malaria or bovine babesiosis metabolomics at the pathway level. Not suitable for severity, outcome or treatment-response questions, nor for prognostic modelling.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are more defensible than ML here: per-feature Welch or Mann-Whitney tests (or linear models on log-transformed intensities) with Benjamini-Hochberg FDR, adjusted for age and sex where covariates can be obtained; effect sizes with bootstrap confidence intervals; a multivariate check (PCA, PLS-DA with permutation testing, or penalised logistic regression with nested leave-one-out or repeated CV, with feature selection inside each fold). Exact or permutation tests for the few-sample comparisons; pathway analysis treated as exploratory. Any clustering is exploratory and must not be called subtypes. A classifier is not defensible with n=24 and 1802+205 features, and any AUC would be optimistic without external validation.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n/high p (24 dogs vs 1802 untargeted + 205 targeted features); no external validation cohort. Case-control with confounding by age (infected 8 months-10 years vs controls 1-12 years) and sex, and by anaemia, fever and acute-phase response, so effects cannot be attributed to the parasite itself; control matching, recruitment period and co-infection screening are not stated. Selection bias (hospital-presenting clinical cases vs healthy controls), and no severity grading. Batch effects cannot be assessed: run order and injection batches are not given, and case/control run order is unknown, although pooled QC samples exist (QC data not included). Outcome leakage: the samples were taken pre-treatment, so there is no post-treatment leakage, but the t-test selection and pathway analyses in S1B/S2B/S3 used all 24 dogs, so any classifier built on those preselected features would be leaky. Repeated platforms on the same dogs must be split at the animal level (one dog, three platforms). No repeated measures over time. Units, normalisation and the LOD handling of NA values are not documented, and S2A rows lack IDs, so linkage across platforms is by row order only. The GC-MS data are absent. Cross-species comparability is limited because the targeted panel (a commercial kit validated for human plasma) and annotation rely on human databases (KEGG, HMDB); canine serum differs from human plasma matrix.

**Unverified fields:** S2A row-to-dog-ID linkage (rows have no sample IDs; assumed same order as S4); Concentration units in S2A; Normalisation/transformation applied to S1A intensities; Meaning of NA in S2A (LOD vs missing at random); Deduplicated count of identified untargeted metabolites (article says 64); Exact row counts of S1B, S1C, S2B, S3A, S3B (approximate from the sheet dimensions); GC-MS data table (absent from the supplement); Age, sex and clinical covariates per dog (only in article text, not in the files); Whether raw MS data are deposited in any repository (article says on request); Control matching, run order and batch structure; Overlap with other Zagreb group cohorts

**Downloaded files (1; 1.22 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB137/PMC12024595_supplementaryFiles.zip` (1,217,449 B, sha256 `5c535c7aa098…`)

**Suitable questions:** Case-control comparison of salivary metabolite abundance (infected vs healthy) as hypothesis-generating evidence; exploring purine, urea-cycle, amino-acid and lipid shifts; relationship of salivary creatinine to systemic azotaemia (needs serum values, which are not in the files); descriptive assessment of annotation coverage and of which features were standard-matched (Table S2). Not suitable for severity, outcome, prognostic, treatment-response or longitudinal questions, or for external diagnostic validation (no independent cohort).

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are more defensible than ML at n=28 (14 vs 14) with p about 2257 untargeted or 39 targeted variables. Use log-transformed intensities, Wilcoxon/Welch tests or linear models per feature with Benjamini-Hochberg FDR and effect sizes with confidence intervals, plus exact or permutation tests; censored-data methods (Tobit, left-censored imputation) or presence/absence models for S3 NA; penalised regression (elastic net/lasso) with nested cross-validation and permutation null only for exploratory ranking, and any classifier performance reported as optimistic. PCA/PLS-DA are descriptive only; PLS-DA at this p/n overfits, so use permutation tests. Do not describe any clusters as subtypes.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n/high p (28 samples, 2257 features): high false-discovery and unstable ML. Case-control with healthy controls only: no other-disease controls, so signal may reflect generic illness, azotaemia (salivary creatinine tracked serum creatinine per article), hydration, fasting or diet rather than Babesia-specific biology. Selection/treatment confounding: infected dogs are clinic cases, treatment (imidocarb) timing relative to sampling is uncontrolled, and age, sex, breed and co-infection are absent from the files. Possible batch/run-order effects are not documented (no QC, injection order, or normalisation info); sample-specific missingness in S1 (Uzorak_6, 9, 10) suggests analytical quality variation in some Babesia samples, and Uzorak_9 shows markedly lower intensities in sample rows inspected, which may reflect sample quality. Outcome leakage: the only label is the group itself, and annotation or 'significant feature' lists in the article were selected on this label, so any feature selection must be done inside cross-validation. Animal-level splitting: one sample per dog, so no repeated-measure leakage, but no dog IDs are given to confirm this. S3 is about 21% NA with undocumented censoring and a repeated His value of 176 in 22 samples (apparent floor or imputation). Units absent. Cross-species comparability: saliva, untargeted Orbitrap features with probabilistic annotation only; direct comparison to human or other species serum/plasma metabolomics is weak and needs identity-matched metabolites (S2 or standards only). No severity or outcome labels, so no mechanistic inference on pathophysiology beyond case-control associations. Table S4 pathway enrichment is not significant after Holm or FDR correction (Holm P=1, FDR 0.98-1).

**Unverified fields:** Whether each dog contributed exactly one sample (no dog IDs in files; single time point taken from article note); Mapping of Table S1 sample columns (Uzorak_1..28) to Table S3 Sample/Label rows; Units for S1 intensities and S3 concentrations; Meaning of S3 'H1' and 'xLeu' and of the repeated His=176 value; Normalisation, QC, batch and run-order information; Count of 868 significant features and seven significant targeted metabolites (not marked in the files); Exact count of the seven metabolites confirmed with reference standards versus the 36 'identified' rows in Table S2; Table S4 content beyond the 5 enrichment rows shown by the helper (rows 7 reported including header and title); Whether the supplement carries a licence separate from the article's CC BY; Overlap of these dogs with other Zagreb babesiosis datasets or articles

(4) Content matches the article's findings: phospho-SAA S101 (SAA 'Phospho [S101]' rows with 'High' in all 5 babesia samples in both babesia exports, absent in control exports), kininogen-1 pT326, fibrinogen alpha, fetuin-A (alpha-2-HS-glycoprotein), canine accessions (Canis lupus familiaris). (5) Supplement S1 Table: 10 controls (H1-H10) and 10 babesiosis dogs (B1-B10) with breed/age/sex and hematology, plus 'Significant phosphopeptides' sheet (SAA pS102 [article says S101], kininogen pT326, fibrinogen alpha pT20/pS22) with p-values .00018/.02574/.02574/.01928, matching the article note (SAA p=0.00018). Europe PMC supplement file names pone.0207245.s001/s002/s003 correspond to the article's S1 Table, S2 Table, S1 Fig. Caveat: the PRIDE sample labels (e.g. B6, B49, B24, B106, B189, B75, B76, B11, B12, B47; K1S, K2S, K4S, K5, K5J, k5, K7, K8, K10, K10S) are NOT mapped to the article's S1 IDs (H1-H10, B1-B10), so dog-level linkage between clinical/phosphopeptide values and the LC-MS exports is not documented. Minor internal inconsistencies: sheet lists control F6 (20161114_Total_k5) while the 1122 control export header shows F20 instead of F6; sample name '20131125_TOTAL_B49' likely a year typo. |
| Repository / accession | PRIDE (EMBL-EBI) PXD010894 for omics files; Europe PMC (PMC6261647) for PLOS ONE supplementary files / PXD010894; article DOI 10.1371/journal.pone.0207245; PMCID PMC6261647 |
| Landing page | https://www.ebi.ac.uk/pride/archive/projects/PXD010894 |
| Licence / reuse terms | Article and its supplements: CC BY (Europe PMC REST core record for PMC6261647: license = 'cc by', isOpenAccess = Y; read 2026-10-23). PRIDE deposit PXD010894: no dataset-specific licence seen in the downloaded files or manifest; only the repository's general EBI/PRIDE data terms of use apply (not logged in, no terms accepted; PRIDE record page itself not read for a licence statement). |
| Species | Domestic dog (Canis lupus familiaris), serum; naturally infected with Babesia canis; Croatia (Zagreb) |
| Design | Cross-sectional case-control serum phosphoproteomics at a single time point: 10 healthy dogs vs 10 dogs with uncomplicated B. canis babesiosis (SIRS-positive, no organ dysfunction per the article note). Gel-free LC-MS/MS of 20 individual sera after phosphopeptide enrichment (Sequest HT/Proteome Discoverer; run in two batches of 5 babesia + 5 control, 22 Nov and 8 Dec 2016 files), plus 6-plex TMT of pooled samples (not-enriched and enriched/flow-through runs; channel 128 = control pool). Supplement S1 adds per-dog breed, age, sex, hematology and quantified intensities for 4 significant phosphopeptides. |
| Granularity | individual dog/sample-level, processed omics, summary statistics only |
| Raw vs processed | Processed only in the folder. Downloaded: Proteome Discoverer exports (TargetProtein lists for 4 individual-sample batches; TMT protein, peptide-group and PSM exports) with per-sample detection flags ('High'/'Medium'/'Not Found'), not intensity matrices, for the 20 individual samples; TMT files have reporter ratios vs the control channel for pooled samples. S1 Table has quantified per-dog intensities (very small values, apparently normalised; unit/normalisation not stated) for only 4 phosphopeptides and hematology per dog. S2 Table is a formatted report (MS parameters/spectral lists), not a tidy table. RAW files (~22.6 GB PRIDE record) were not downloaded; no peptide/phosphosite-level quantitative matrix for individual dogs is provided. 'Summary statistics only' applies to the TMT pool ratios and p-values in S1 sheet 2. |
| Independent animals | 20 — Counted from data: Sampleinfophosphoresultfiles.xlsx has 20 unique sample/file IDs and 20 unique sample names (10 'B' babesia, 10 'K' control labels); S1 Table has 20 unique IDs (H1-H10, B1-B10). Both agree with 10 vs 10. Caveat: control labels k5/K5/K5J (and K10S/K10) look similar, so that each is a distinct dog is assumed from the 10 control runs and the article, not proven; PRIDE labels cannot be mapped to S1 IDs, so the two 20-dog lists are not verifiably the same dogs. TMT pool membership (which dogs are in which pool or channel) is not documented in the files ('TMT samples: control was ...' cell is truncated). |
| Samples | 20 |
| Groups and controls | Two groups: B. canis babesiosis (n=10; uncomplicated, SIRS-positive, no organ dysfunction per article) vs healthy controls (n=10; per article note controls received prophylactic imidocarb, ages 1-10 y; infected 0.3-13 y in S1). No non-babesial inflammatory/disease control and no severity gradient. S1: infected dogs are marked thrombocytopenic (17-111 x10^9/L) and anaemic (HCT 27-50%); controls normal. Sex: M, F, MC (castrated male), FS (sterilised female) coded in S1 (legend lists 'MS' for sterilised female, mismatch with 'FS' in data). |
| Time points | Single time point per dog (one serum sample, presumed at admission/before treatment; exact timing relative to imidocarb not verified from files). No longitudinal data. Run dates (Nov-Dec 2016) are instrument batches, not biological time points. |
| Features | Individual-sample exports: 176-393 protein rows each (176/207 control, 393/343 babesia; 25 columns incl. accession, description, coverage, #peptides, #PSMs, FidoCT/Sequest HT scores, per-sample 'Found' flag, 'Modifications' with phosphosite and ptmRS localisation); only about 42-54 rows per file carry a Phospho modification (~50-55 phosphoproteins reported in article). TMT: 589 proteins (37 columns, 19 phospho rows), 1,944 peptide groups (35 cols, 6 phospho), 5,768 PSMs (51 cols, 21 phospho), reporter ratios 126,127,129,130,131 vs 128, ratio SEs. S1 Table: per dog age (years), breed, sex, leukocytes, lymphocytes, nonsegmented and segmented neutrophils, monocytes, eosinophils, thrombocytes, HCT, erythrocytes (9 hematology variables); intensities for 4 phosphopeptides (SAA pS102/S101, kininogen-1 pT326, fibrinogen alpha pT20, pS22) per dog. S2: MS parameters per phosphopeptide (sequence, charge, m/z, delta score, q-value, site probabilities). Biochemistry reference ranges (urea, creatinine, protein, albumin, glucose, AST, ALT, GGT, ALP, CK, LDH, amylase, lipase, cholesterol, triglycerides, CRP) are listed but NO values are given. |
| Outcomes | None (no survival, severity, complication or treatment-response labels). Only group label (babesiosis vs healthy); article note says all infected dogs are uncomplicated. |
| Data dictionary | Partial. S1 has headers and reference ranges (with some units: g/L, mmol/L, U/L, x10^9/L, %), but units for the hematology columns are not stated on the data columns and are inconsistent between groups (controls: lymphocytes/neutrophils/monocytes/eosinophils as absolute counts, e.g. lymphocytes 1.6-5.3; babesiosis dogs: lymphocytes 11-45 and segmented neutrophils 50-83, apparently percentages), the units/normalisation of phosphopeptide intensities are not stated, and SAA site is labelled pS102 in S1 versus S101 in S2/article. PRIDE sample sheet gives only filename-to-sample-label mapping (no dictionary); Proteome Discoverer column names are standard but undocumented in the folder. No mapping between PRIDE labels and S1 IDs. |
| Missingness | Clinical S1 table: no blanks in the 20 x 9 hematology block. Phosphopeptide sheet: zeros used for non-detection (e.g. SAA pS102 = 0 in all 10 controls; fibrinogen alpha pT20 = 0 in 3/10 controls and 7/10 infected), so detection-limited/structural zeros, not true values. PRIDE exports: 'Not Found' fraction in per-sample flags is high and group-dependent (about 49% and 26% in the two babesia files, 19% and 29% in the two control files, across all listed proteins); TMT 56% and 55% of channel flags Not Found and about 65-72% of ratio cells blank. Biochemistry values entirely absent. |
| Technical requirements | Spreadsheet/tab-delimited reading (Excel XLSX, tab-delimited text, latin-1 encoding needed for some Proteome Discoverer exports; non-ASCII characters present). No genome annotation needed; protein accessions are NCBI GI/UniProt IDs for dog (Canis lupus familiaris) and some human UniProt IDs (e.g. P02768 albumin), so mapping to current UniProt/Ensembl CanFam would be required to harmonise. Re-quantification would need the 22.6 GB RAW files plus Proteome Discoverer (Sequest HT, ptmRS) or an open alternative (MaxQuant/FragPipe), which is not available in the folder. S2 requires manual table parsing (stacked blocks, comma decimal marks and truncated m/z values in places). |
| Files | data/raw/DS09 (7 text exports + 1 xlsx, about 4.3 MB total): 4 individual-sample TargetProtein .txt (116 kB, 51 kB, 98 kB, 60 kB), TMT TargetProtein .txt (215 kB), TMT TargetPeptideGroup .txt (651 kB), TMT TargetPeptideSpectrumMatch .txt (3.2 MB), Sampleinfophosphoresultfiles.xlsx (10.5 kB; sample sheet on Sheet1, Sheet2/3 empty). data/raw/S-CB019 (1.18 MB zip): s001.xlsx (S1 Table, 2 sheets), s002.xlsx (S2 Table, 3 sheets), s003.docx (S1 Fig), plus figure/table images (jpg/gif). RAW LC-MS/MS files (~22.6 GB) exist only at PRIDE and were not downloaded. |
| Overlaps | Same 20 dogs as CB019 only (no other article). The 20 PRIDE runs, S1 clinical values, S1 phosphopeptide intensities and the TMT pools all derive from the same cohort, so they are not independent datasets. Possible overlap of the TMT pools with the individual dogs (pooled from the same sera). Likely overlap in dogs/cohort with other Zagreb group serum proteomic/metabolomic studies of B. canis (unverified). No repeated measures within dogs (single sample each); the per-sample detection flags appear in both a protein export and TMT, which duplicate information. |

**Downloaded files (9; 5.59 MB):** `data/raw/DS09/20161122_babesia_5samples_TargetProtein.txt` (116,321 B, sha256 `cd605166d051…`); `data/raw/DS09/20161122_control_5samples_total_TargetProtein.txt` (51,159 B, sha256 `c6c4d8492953…`); `data/raw/DS09/20161208_babesia_5samples_TargetProtein.txt` (97,926 B, sha256 `ca26a2aa7114…`); `data/raw/DS09/Sampleinfophosphoresultfiles.xlsx` (10,553 B, sha256 `b4d8528b8959…`); `data/raw/DS09/20170323_tmt_phosposerum_BeforeEnrichment_TargetProtein.txt` (215,192 B, sha256 `1e0f763c19d0…`); `data/raw/DS09/20170405_TMT_nonphospho_after_enrichment_TargetPeptideGroup.txt` (651,282 B, sha256 `d48b041dbf11…`); `data/raw/DS09/20161208_control_5samples_total_TargetProtein.txt` (60,495 B, sha256 `4e1c461559ca…`); `data/raw/DS09/20170405_TMT_nonphospho_after_enrichment_TargetPeptideSpectrumMatch.txt` (3,215,616 B, sha256 `74cdfa57956b…`); … 1 more in the manifest

**Suitable questions:** (a) Reproduce/extend group differences in detection of the 4 phosphopeptides (SAA pS101/102, kininogen-1 pT326, fibrinogen alpha pT20/pS22) in 10 vs 10 dogs from S1; (b) Descriptive catalogue of canine serum phosphosites and acute-phase/coagulation/kallikrein-kinin phosphoproteins (SAA, fetuin-A, kininogen-1, fibrinogen, complement factor H, apolipoprotein A-I) for mechanism hypothesis-generation; (c) Correlation of phosphopeptide intensities with hematology (thrombocytes, HCT, leukocytes) within infected dogs (descriptive only, n=10); (d) Presence/absence comparison of phosphoproteins between groups from the Proteome Discoverer exports; (e) Context for cross-species comparison (qualitative: SAA phosphorylation, fibrinogen/kininogen phosphorylation vs human sepsis/malaria literature). Not suitable for severity prediction, outcome modelling, subtype discovery or time-course questions.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are far more defensible than ML here: exact tests (Fisher exact for detection/non-detection, e.g. SAA pS101 10/10 vs 0/10), Mann-Whitney or permutation tests with Benjamini-Hochberg across phosphopeptides, effect sizes with bootstrap CIs, rank correlations of phosphopeptide intensity vs hematology (state units fix first), and for any multi-feature work penalised regression/logistic with nested CV is a last resort with explicit acknowledgement of n=20. Treat batches (22 Nov vs 8 Dec run) as a blocking factor (each batch has 5 vs 5, so balanced by design) in a stratified/paired permutation test. Do not run unsupervised clustering and label results as subtypes; no outcome variable exists for supervised learning.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n/high p: n=10 per group with hundreds of proteins and about 50 phosphoproteins, only four phosphopeptides have per-dog quantitative values; ML would overfit and validation is infeasible (no independent cohort; leave-one-dog-out CV would be the only option and is unstable). Single time point, no outcomes or severity (no gradient; only uncomplicated disease), so no prognostic or severity use and no outcome leakage as such, but the group label is confounded with disease state, thrombocytopenia/anaemia, age (infected 0.3-13 y vs controls 1-13 y; many young dogs in the infected group), breed and sex. Treatment confounding: controls reportedly given prophylactic imidocarb and infected dogs treated at admission, sampling time versus treatment unverified. Selection confounding: infected dogs selected as SIRS-positive; healthy controls not matched; no non-babesial inflammatory disease control, so phospho-SAA could be generic acute-phase. Batch: two LC-MS/MS batches (different dates, even different file dates for one control, F6 20161114) and the TMT run are separate; detection flags are strongly dependent on protein abundance and depth (SAA is abundant in infected dogs, so presence/absence may reflect parent protein abundance, not stoichiometry). Detection flags, not intensities, in PRIDE exports; per-sample missingness high and group-dependent. Repeated measures not applicable (one sample per dog), but TMT pools hide individual dogs and cannot be split at the animal level. No ID mapping between PRIDE and S1; incongruities (F6 vs F20; pS102 vs S101; unit mismatch of leukocyte differentials between groups; 'MS' vs 'FS' code; truncated TMT-sample note) require manual verification. Cross-species: canine accessions and gi-based IDs, isoform redundancy (multiple SAA, fibronectin, kininogen isoforms) inflate protein counts; mapping to human phosphosites would need alignment and is not provided; phosphoproteome of serum is dominated by a few proteins (fetuin-A 50-70%).

**Unverified fields:** PRIDE dataset-specific licence (record page not read); mapping between PRIDE sample labels (B6, B49, K5, etc.) and S1 IDs (H1-H10, B1-B10); whether control labels k5/K5/K5J and K10/K10S are distinct dogs; pool composition and channel assignments of the TMT runs; timing of serum sampling relative to imidocarb treatment; RAW file count and sizes (not downloaded; ~22.6 GB taken from task metadata); units and normalisation of S1 phosphopeptide intensities; article details taken from the CB019 note (SIRS criteria, imidocarb prophylaxis in controls, no organ dysfunction) not re-checked against the full text; hematology unit interpretation (absolute vs percent) for the babesiosis group
