### DS08 — PRIDE PXD047647: serum extracellular vesicle proteomics, healthy vs Babesia canis-infected dogs (partial download: sample sheet plus 7 Proteome Discoverer exports)

**Why ranked 16:** Serum extracellular-vesicle proteome (B. canis vs healthy); processed exports only partly downloaded.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Public download verified |
| Linked articles | [CB122](articles/CB122_2024_characterization-lc-ms-ms-based-proteomic.md) |
| Correspondence check | Files correspond to CB122 (Rešetar Maslov et al., Vet Parasitol 2024; PRIDE PXD047647 cited in the article note) in design and chemistry, but not in sample number. Samples.xlsx lists two groups, 'Healthy' and 'Babesia canis'. Protein exports are Proteome Discoverer tables (Sequest HT, canine UniProt, OX=9615), matching the note's 'PD 2.3, canine reference proteome, SEC fractions 2 and 3'. Sample names carry suffixes _2 and _3, consistent with the two SEC fractions. Sample counts: Samples.xlsx has 40 samples = 10 distinct animal IDs per group x 2 fractions. The note says the abstract reports 15 per group and the PRIDE protocol says 10 per group (the repository record also carries 40 samples). So the data match the PRIDE protocol (10 per group), not the 15 per group in the abstract. The abstract's 211 proteins / 147 vesicle-validated / 73 infected-specific cannot be checked because only 7 of 40 per-sample exports are present and no group-level table was downloaded. Variables, time points and severity are not in the files, so the comparison is limited to n, groups and the fraction structure. Article full text was not accessible (paywalled), so correspondence rests on the PRIDE ID link, group labels, search software and sample count. |

| Licence / reuse terms | Not specified at dataset level. No dataset-specific licence appeared in the downloaded files or the retrieved metadata. The general PRIDE/EMBL-EBI terms of use for public data apply. I did not check the licence page this session, so the exact terms (for example whether CC0 applies) are unverified. No terms were accepted and no login was used. Europe PMC check not applicable because the article has no PMCID. |
| Species | Domestic dog (Canis lupus familiaris); host proteins searched against canine UniProt (OX=9615). Pathogen: Babesia canis (suspected; identification method not reported in the note). Few parasite proteins passed the article's filters. |
| Design | Case-control (naturally infected vs healthy) exploratory shotgun proteomics of serum small extracellular vesicles isolated by size-exclusion chromatography (fractions 2 and 3), data-dependent nano-LC-MS/MS, Proteome Discoverer 2.3. Described as a preliminary study. Cross-sectional: one serum collection per dog, no follow-up. Of the full submission (about 19 GB: RAW and search files for 40 samples), only the sample sheet and 7 processed export tables were retrieved. |
| Granularity | processed omics |
| Raw vs processed | Processed only. The downloaded files are Proteome Discoverer per-sample identification exports: 6 protein tables (Proteins.txt) and 1 protein-group table (ProteinGroups.txt). They contain identification metrics (coverage, peptide and PSM counts, Sequest score) and no quantitative abundance columns (no intensities or area, no normalised values). RAW files and other search outputs were not downloaded. No group-level quantitative matrix is present, so the data are usable for presence/absence and identification-depth comparisons, not for differential abundance, unless the RAW files are reprocessed. |
| Independent animals | 20 — Counted from Samples.xlsx. The sheet lists 40 sample names (egzosomi_N_2 and egzosomi_N_3). There are 20 distinct N values: Healthy 1,2,3,4,5,6,7,15,17,18 (10) and B. canis 8,9,10,12,24,25,26,27,28,29 (10). I assume N is the animal ID and the suffix is the SEC fraction. Neither assumption is documented in a data dictionary; the fraction reading rests on the article note. The figure of 20 is therefore an inference from naming, not a verified animal count. The abstract states 15 per group (30 dogs), which the files do not support. |
| Samples | 40 |
| Groups and controls | Two groups: Healthy (10 IDs, 20 samples) and Babesia canis (10 IDs, 20 samples). Healthy dogs act as controls. Inclusion criteria, infection confirmation method, parasitaemia, severity, co-infections, treatment status, age, breed, sex and sampling context are not in the files. Only 7 of 40 samples have exports: 5 healthy (2_3, 3_3, 4_3, 5_2, 17_3; 5 distinct dogs) and 2 B. canis (9_2 as a protein-group table, 12_2; 2 distinct dogs). That is 7 samples from 7 dogs, so a case-control comparison on the downloaded files alone is hugely unbalanced (5 vs 2) and underpowered. |
| Time points | None. A single serum sample per dog (cross-sectional). The _2/_3 suffix is SEC fraction, not time, so there are no repeated measures over time but there are 2 technical or fractionation replicates per dog (as inferred). |
| Features | Per-sample Proteins.txt tables, 14 columns: Checked, Master (master-protein status), Accession (UniProt), Description (name, OS, OX=9615, GN, PE, SV), Coverage [%], # Peptides, # PSMs, # Unique Peptides, # AAs, MW [kDa], calc. pI, Sequest HT score, # Peptides by search engine, # Protein Groups. Protein rows per file: 12_2 = 36; 2_3 = 132; 3_3 = 110; 4_3 = 430; 5_2 = 647; 17_3 = 1046 (all 'Checked' = False). The protein-group file (9_2) has 14 groups and 7 columns (Checked, Protein Group ID, # Proteins, # Unique Peptides, # Peptides, # PSMs, Group Description). Row counts cover all master-status categories: files 2_3, 3_3, 12_2 contain only IsMasterProtein rows, while 17_3, 4_3 and 5_2 also contain Candidate, Rejected and None rows, so the files were exported under different filter settings and are not directly comparable. Samples.xlsx has 2 columns (group, sample name). No quantitative abundance features. |
| Outcomes | None. There are no clinical outcome, severity, survival or laboratory variables. The only label is group (Healthy vs Babesia canis). |
| Data dictionary | Absent. No readme or column dictionary is in the folder. The Proteome Discoverer column names are self-describing (units given in headers for coverage % and MW kDa). The meaning of the sample-name pattern egzosomi_N_k (dog vs fraction) and the export filters used are undocumented; they come only from the article note and the PRIDE protocol. |
| Missingness | No empty cells in any of the 7 tables or the sample sheet (the group label in Samples.xlsx is entered only on the first row of each group, a merged-cell style, not missing data). Absence of a protein from a sample's table is informative missingness (below detection or filtered) and is strongly dependent on the export filters. Counts of 36 to 1046 proteins per file confirm uneven depth and filtering across samples. 33 of 40 samples have no data in the folder. |
| Technical requirements | Tab-delimited text (UTF-8 with CRLF, quoted fields) readable in any tool. Samples.xlsx needs a spreadsheet reader. Mapping to gene or pathway uses UniProt accessions (canine reference proteome, OX=9615; many 'Uncharacterized protein' entries, LOC/ENSEMBL-style gene names), so orthology mapping to human is needed for pathway work. Reprocessing from RAW needs Proteome Discoverer 2.3 or an open alternative (MaxQuant, FragPipe) plus the canine FASTA and substantial compute and storage (about 19 GB for the full record). The 8 downloaded files total about 0.43 MB. |
| Files | 8 files in data/raw/DS08/: Samples.xlsx (11,600 bytes); egzosomi_12_2_Proteins.txt (6,308 B; 36 rows); egzosomi_2_3_Proteins.txt (22,643 B; 132 rows); egzosomi_3_3_Proteins.txt (18,891 B; 110 rows); egzosomi_4_3_Proteins.txt (73,998 B; 430 rows); egzosomi_5_2_Proteins.txt (113,134 B; 647 rows); egzosomi_17_3_Proteins.txt (185,376 B; 1,046 rows); egzosomi_9_2_ProteinGroups.txt (1,866 B; 14 groups). SHA-256 values are in data/manifest.jsonl. Not downloaded: RAW files, remaining search outputs for 33 samples (about 19 GB record in total). |
| Overlaps | The same cohort as CB122 only. No other dataset in the review is linked to it. Each dog contributes two SEC-fraction samples (inferred), so samples are not independent. The article abstract (15 per group) and repository (10 per group) disagree, and the instrument model is listed inconsistently (Q Exactive HF vs Plus) in the record, which is a provenance concern but does not affect the downloaded tables. |

**Downloaded files (8; 0.43 MB):** `data/raw/DS08/egzosomi_9_2_ProteinGroups.txt` (1,866 B, sha256 `6250fd5e284c…`); `data/raw/DS08/egzosomi_5_2_Proteins.txt` (113,134 B, sha256 `df7c76139612…`); `data/raw/DS08/egzosomi_17_3_Proteins.txt` (185,376 B, sha256 `d31a26f9d51d…`); `data/raw/DS08/egzosomi_2_3_Proteins.txt` (22,643 B, sha256 `05068858c8fe…`); `data/raw/DS08/egzosomi_3_3_Proteins.txt` (18,891 B, sha256 `09f97c055d95…`); `data/raw/DS08/Samples.xlsx` (11,600 B, sha256 `00dffcc5c640…`); `data/raw/DS08/egzosomi_4_3_Proteins.txt` (73,998 B, sha256 `0b5bfbc41a51…`); `data/raw/DS08/egzosomi_12_2_Proteins.txt` (6,308 B, sha256 `4253fe6f4722…`)

**Suitable questions:** With the downloaded files only: descriptive checks (which proteins are detected in healthy vs infected EV preparations, for example apolipoproteins, complement, immunoglobulins, fibronectin), a sanity check against the article's protein lists, and a feasibility assessment for reanalysis. The 7 exports (5 healthy, 2 infected, one a group-level table) cannot support any inferential group comparison. With the full submission (40 RAW or search files) plus quantification: exploratory differential abundance of EV proteins in B. canis vs healthy dogs, testing whether platelet, immune-cell and integrin/VEGF-annotated EV cargo differs; fraction (SEC 2 vs 3) differences. The dataset cannot address severity, complicated vs uncomplicated babesiosis, outcome prediction, time course or treatment response, because no phenotype data exist beyond infected vs healthy.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are more defensible than ML. If full quantitative data are reprocessed: per-protein linear mixed models with dog as random effect (to handle the two SEC fractions per dog), or analyse fractions separately and treat dog as the unit; Fisher exact or permutation tests for detection frequency; moderated tests (limma-style) with multiple-testing control; penalised regression with nested, animal-level cross-validation only as an exploratory check (about 20 dogs is far too few for a trustworthy classifier). On the downloaded subset, report descriptives only (no tests). Exploratory clusters must not be described as subtypes.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n, high p: at most 10 dogs per group (20 total) against hundreds of proteins; the downloaded subset is 5 vs 2 dogs. Repeated measures: two SEC-fraction samples per dog (inferred) must be split or modelled by animal, never at sample level, to avoid leakage in cross-validation. Batch and depth effects: protein counts per file range 36 to 1046 and files use different master-status filters, so identification-depth differences confound group comparisons; batch, run order and processing date are not recorded. Selection and confounding: healthy and infected dogs may differ in age, breed, sex, hydration, lipaemia and treatment, none of which are documented; infection confirmation and species assignment (B. canis) are not described; co-infections and prior treatment unknown. Identification-only data: no abundance, so detection is a function of sampling depth; serum contamination with abundant plasma proteins (APOB, immunoglobulins, complement) is visible and means a vesicle-specific interpretation needs the article's validation filter (147 of 211). No outcome or severity labels, so no outcome leakage risk but also no clinical-endpoint use. Validation: no independent cohort; the sample-size discrepancy between abstract (15 per group) and repository (10 per group) is unresolved. Cross-species comparability: canine UniProt annotation is incomplete (many uncharacterised proteins), so mapping to human or murine pathways loses information; the dataset is useful for host-response context but not as a severity model.

**Unverified fields:** Whether N in egzosomi_N_k is the dog ID and k the SEC fraction (assumed from the article note and the naming pattern); Total number of independent animals (20 inferred from names; abstract states 30); Which sample belongs to which dog across fractions; Correspondence of the 211/147/73 protein counts to the downloaded tables; Dataset-specific licence and exact PRIDE/EMBL-EBI terms; Infection confirmation method, Babesia species identification, clinical severity, co-infections, treatment status, demographics; Export filter settings used for each Proteins.txt file; Content of the 33 samples and the RAW files that were not downloaded; Instrument model (Q Exactive HF vs Plus); Full article text (paywalled; not read)

<a id="s-cb042"></a>
### S-CB042 — Table S1: Clinical data from B. vulpes-infected dogs (JVIM 2019;33:2075-2081, Barash et al.)

**Why ranked 17:** B. vulpes clinical data from North American dogs; sparse clinicopathology, heavy co-infection.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Public download verified |
| Linked articles | [CB042](articles/CB042_2019_prevalence-babesia-spp-clinical-characteristics-babesia.md) |
| Correspondence check | Verified against the CB042 note. Sheet 'B vulpes' has 48 unique Animal IDs (1-48), matching the 48 B. vulpes-positive dogs. Babesia Infection(s) column gives 19 'Bv_only' and 29 'Bv_Bgib', matching '19 sole agent, 29 with B. gibsoni'. 44 of 48 rows are American Staffordshire Terrier (pit bull-type) and 4 are other breeds (dachshund, beagle, schnauzer, terrier), matching the full-text 44/48. Hematocrit is present for 14 dogs, matching 'clinicopathology for 14'. Treatment-clearance columns are populated for 9 dogs, matching 'treatment follow-up in 9 dogs'. One dog from Calgary (Canada) matches 'one sample from Calgary'. NY-dominated geography and the dog-fighting column fit the article. The 9367-sample prevalence survey (269 PCR-positive) is not in this file. The zip also contains Figure 1 and 2 images (JVIM-33-2075-g001/g002 gif and jpg), which were not examined as data. |
| Repository / accession | Europe PMC / PubMed Central supplementary files (journal supplement, JVIM) / PMC6766513; article DOI 10.1111/jvim.15560; file JVIM-33-2075-s001.xlsx |
| Landing page | https://europepmc.org/article/PMC/PMC6766513 |
| Licence / reuse terms | CC BY-NC (Europe PMC core record field 'license' = 'cc by-nc', isOpenAccess = Y; read from the Europe PMC REST search API on 2026-10-23). This is the article licence. The supplement is assumed to follow it, and no separate supplement-specific licence was seen. Non-commercial use only. |
| Species | Domestic dog (Canis lupus familiaris); naturally infected with Babesia vulpes (with or without B. gibsoni) |
| Design | Per-animal descriptive case series from a convenience sample of reference-laboratory PCR submissions (NCSU Vector-Borne Disease Diagnostic Laboratory), 2015-2018. One row per B. vulpes PCR-positive dog. Mostly US (NY-heavy), one dog from Calgary, Canada. Data were supplied by submitting clinicians, so they are heterogeneous and retrospective. No prospective protocol. |
| Granularity | individual dog/sample-level |
| Raw vs processed | Individual-level observed values (raw clinical and clinicopathology values with lab-specific reference ranges), not summary statistics. Values are transcribed into a spreadsheet and some are coarsened: age as free text or ranges, proteinuria as semi-quantitative grades, clearance as free-text strings. The colour coding (red/blue/gold) is a visual layer and was not machine-read. |
| Independent animals | 48 — Counted from the data: 48 data rows with 48 unique Animal IDs (1-48), one row per dog. No duplicated IDs. Independence between dogs is not guaranteed because several NY/Ithaca fighting-dog rows may share kennels or an outbreak. |
| Samples | 48 |
| Groups and controls | Two infection groups: Bv_only n=19 and Bv_Bgib (B. vulpes plus B. gibsoni DNA-positive) n=29. There is no uninfected control group, so no healthy or disease controls. Mycoplasma co-infection is recorded in about 29 dogs (M. haemocanis or Candidatus M. haematoparvum), plus other co-infections (Ehrlichia IFA, Bartonella, Wolbachia, Rickettsia). Dog fighting or aggression is yes for most of the dogs with a recorded value. |
| Time points | Essentially cross-sectional at diagnosis. A single time point per dog for clinicopathology. Only 9 dogs have post-treatment PCR clearance information, as free text ('negative at 30, 60, 90 days', 'positive at 3 and 4 months'), which is not a structured time series. |
| Features | 43 columns. Signalment and epidemiology: city, state, breed, age (years, free text including ranges and 'UNK'), sex, dog fighting/aggression, bite wounds/scars, splenectomy. Serology and PCR: B. canis IFA, B. gibsoni IFA, B. gibsoni DNA, infection group. Co-infection: Mycoplasma, other co-infections. Treatment response: Bml (B. vulpes) cleared, Bgib cleared. Clinical: temperature (F), splenomegaly. Clinicopathology, each with its adjacent reference-range column: Hct, reticulocytes, platelets, neutrophils, eosinophils, WBC, globulins, albumin, creatinine, BUN, bilirubin. Urine: proteinuria grade, USG. Units are not stated in the sheet (for example Hct as % or fraction, platelets x10^3/uL, retic count mixed). About 11 distinct lab analytes. |
| Outcomes | No survival or severity labels. The only outcome-like fields are clearance of infection after treatment (9 dogs; Bml cleared and Bgib cleared, free text). The article states all 9 treated dogs improved clinically, but that is not coded in the sheet. Anaemia, thrombocytopenia and similar findings can be derived by comparing each value to its reference-range column. |
| Data dictionary | Partial. Column headers are self-explanatory and the sheet header notes explain the abbreviations (Bv, Bgib) and the colour coding. The sheet has no units, no code book (na vs n/a vs UNK vs blank are used inconsistently) and no assay or reference-laboratory specification. Reference ranges differ between laboratories (for example Hct 37.3-61.7, 36-60, 38.3-56.5, 0.38-0.57 as a fraction in the Calgary dog), so the values are not harmonised. |
| Missingness | Very high. Clinicopathology: Hct 14/48 (29%), platelets 12/48, albumin and globulin about 11/48, temperature 5/48, proteinuria and USG about 4/48 (about 8%). Treatment-clearance data in 9/48. Serology (B. canis IFA) is recorded for about 20 dogs. Missing values are coded as na, n/a, UNK or blank. Dogs with clinicopathology are a non-random subset of the case series (clinician submission bias). Several dogs with a Hct entry have blank values for other analytes. |
| Technical requirements | Minimal. One small xlsx opened in Excel, pandas or R. It needs cleaning: age free text parsing, mixed character and numeric cells, reference-range strings ('37.3 - 61.7', en-dashes, '>130', '<0.1'), and lab-specific units for the Calgary dog. Fill colours (colour coding) are only recoverable by reading cell styles. No omics or annotation requirements. |
| Files | PMC6766513_supplementaryFiles.zip, 105,055 bytes (SHA-256 32de6cc0...0930dd16641 per manifest, retrieved 2026-10-23). Members: JVIM-33-2075-s001.xlsx (23,373 bytes; one sheet 'B vulpes', 54 rows x 43 columns: 3 title/legend rows, 1 header row, 48 data rows, plus trailing rows), and figure images JVIM-33-2075-g001.gif/.jpg and g002.gif/.jpg (about 2.9-68 kB each; images were not analysed). |
| Overlaps | The 48 dogs are the same B. vulpes-positive dogs reported in CB042, whose full text says no overlap with earlier publications is stated. Possible overlap with other US B. vulpes / B. gibsoni fighting-dog reports (Birkenheuer / Qurollo / Breitschwerdt group) is not verified. Dogs are one row each, with no repeated measures. Shared kennels and fighting-ring clusters (many Ithaca and New York dogs with identical profiles) imply non-independence. |

**Downloaded files (1; 0.11 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB042/PMC6766513_supplementaryFiles.zip` (105,055 B, sha256 `32de6cc01b83…`)

**Suitable questions:** Descriptive characterisation of the B. vulpes clinicopathological profile (anaemia, thrombocytopenia, hyperglobulinaemia, hypoalbuminaemia, proteinuria) using the n=14 dogs with data. Frequency of co-infection (Mycoplasma, Bartonella, Wolbachia) by infection group. Serology/PCR concordance (B. canis IFA vs Bgib IFA vs Bgib DNA). Bv_only vs Bv_Bgib comparison of the few laboratory values, as hypothesis-generating only. Use as a small illustrative case-series source for the review. Not suitable for modelling severity, outcome, or pathophysiological mechanism, and not suitable as a B. vulpes-versus-control comparison since there is no uninfected group.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary descriptive statistics with exact/Fisher tests, proportions with exact (Clopper-Pearson) confidence intervals, and Wilcoxon or permutation tests for group differences. No ML. With 14 dogs having Hct and about 12 having platelets, and an essentially absent outcome label, no supervised learning or clustering is defensible. If any adjustment is attempted, use penalised or exact logistic regression with very few covariates. Clinicopathology values should be compared against the supplied per-row reference ranges (standardise as below/within/above) rather than pooled raw because ranges and units differ between labs. Do not describe any exploratory grouping as subtypes.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n (48 dogs; 14 with clinicopathology, 4 with urine data, 9 with follow-up). High missingness not missing at random (selection by clinician submission). No controls. Co-infection confounding (haemotropic Mycoplasma, B. gibsoni in 29/48, Bartonella, Wolbachia), which makes it impossible to attribute any finding to B. vulpes alone. Selection and geographic bias (fighting-dog population, NY-centred), with possible non-independence of dogs from shared kennels. Heterogeneous laboratories, reference ranges and units (a different unit scale for the Calgary dog), batch effect by submitting laboratory. Treatment confounding: clearance data derive from different drug regimens that are not recorded in the sheet. Outcome leakage: post-treatment PCR status is a downstream label and 'Bml/Bgib cleared' must not be used as a predictor of itself. Splenectomy status (a known driver of Babesia severity) is mostly missing. No repeated measures, so animal-level splitting is moot, but validation is infeasible beyond cross-validation on a tiny set. Cross-species comparability: canine B. vulpes (an Archaeopiroplasmid/Babesia microti-group parasite) is not directly comparable with B. canis or human B. microti pathophysiology, and the data are descriptive, not mechanistic. Licence is CC BY-NC, so no commercial reuse.

**Unverified fields:** Supplement-specific licence (CC BY-NC read from the article-level Europe PMC record, not from the supplement itself); Units of clinicopathology values (not stated in the sheet); Colour-coding (red/blue/gold) was not machine-read; Exact count of Mycoplasma co-infected dogs and other co-infection counts (approximate, from a visual read of the dump); Treatment regimens and clinical outcomes of the 9 follow-up dogs (not in the sheet); Overlap with other published fighting-dog or B. vulpes cohorts; Contents of the figure images (JVIM-33-2075-g001/g002) were not inspected; Independence of dogs (shared kennels or outbreaks)

### S-CB516 — Eichenberger et al. 2016 Supplemental Files 1 and 2: individual dog descriptions and clinical signs (15 natural B. canis cases) and a differential WBC figure (JVIM-30-174-s001.pdf, -s002.pdf, PMC4913656)

**Why ranked 18:** 15 client-owned B. canis dogs (Switzerland) with travel history, clinical signs and survival (8 died); no laboratory values in machine-readable form.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Public download verified |
| Linked articles | [CB516](articles/CB516_2016_prognostic-markers-acute-babesia-canis-infections.md) |
| Correspondence check | Checked against the article note and the extraction record for CB516. (1) Supplemental File 1 holds 15 numbered dogs. Outcome is 8 "Died" (#1-8) and 7 "Survival" (#9-15), which matches the note (8 nonsurvivors, 7 survivors; 15 natural cases). (2) The note says 9 of 15 natural cases had a travel history. I count 9 with a country (France 4, Hungary 2, Russia 1, Croatia 1, Serbia 1) and 6 marked "no travel history (Switzerland)". This matches. (3) The File 1 caption ("animal description, travel history, and clinical signs") matches the note's description of the file. (4) File 2 caption: differential WBC count in 3 experimentally inoculated dogs (D1-3) and in natural cases split into survivors and nonsurvivors. D1-3 matches the 3 experimental dogs. DISCREPANCY: the caption labels survivors N1-5 (5 dogs) and nonsurvivors N6-11 (6 dogs), which is 11 dogs. The note says 6 survivors and 6 nonsurvivors (12) were used for haematology and biochemistry, and 7 and 8 overall. I cannot tell from the text which dog is missing. (5) File 2 has only the caption in the text dump. The data are in figure panels that were not extracted, so I could not check any WBC value. (6) The note says age, sex and clinical signs did not differ between outcome groups. The File 1 counts look consistent with that (for example pigmenturia 6/8 died vs 4/7 survived; fever 2/8 vs 3/7; icterus 3/8 vs 3/7). I ran no tests. Apathy and pale mucous membranes are present in all 15 dogs. (7) Not in the files: lactate, haematology, biochemistry, CRP/SAA, parasitaemia and the experimental-dog follow-up values. These stay in the article tables only. The note's earlier statement that the supplements were "not opened" is now out of date. |
| Repository / accession | Europe PMC / PMC supplementary files (JVIM Supporting Information) / not specified |
| Landing page | https://europepmc.org/article/PMC/PMC4913656 |
| Licence / reuse terms | article licence (Europe PMC open-access record); not separately specified |
| Species | Dog (Canis lupus familiaris), naturally infected with Babesia canis (File 2 caption: "naturally acquired acute Babesia canis infection"); client-owned dogs of 13 breed labels, Switzerland. File 2 also covers 3 experimentally inoculated dogs (D1-3); the files do not state their breed. |
| Design | Two one-page PDFs. File 2 is a differential WBC count shown as figure panels: (A) the 3 experimental dogs over hours post infection (hpi), (B) natural cases by outcome. The text dump has only the File 2 caption. Zip also has 6 image files (jpg and gif of article figures 1-3, by file name only). |

| Raw vs processed | Processed descriptive table in a PDF. No spreadsheet. File 1 values are recorded observations (age, weight, temperature, yes/no signs), not raw measurements. The 6 image members (g001-g003, each as jpg and gif) were not opened and are presumably article figures (unverified). No raw instrument files. |
| Independent animals | 15 — Counted the numbered rows (#1-15) in File 1: all 15 are naturally infected dogs, 8 Died and 7 Survival. This matches the article note. File 2's caption also names 3 experimental dogs (D1-3) and 11 natural dogs (N1-5 survived, N6-11 did not). These counts come from the caption only, not from data. The 11 natural dogs are presumably a subset of the 15, and the 3 experimental dogs are in addition, so at most 18 distinct dogs are described. n_animals is the 15 rows I could count. |
| Samples | 15 |
| Groups and controls | Two outcome groups in File 1: Died n=8 (#1-8) and Survival n=7 (#9-15). No uninfected control group. No treatment arm. | Time points | File 1: one record per dog. The caption gives no sampling time; the signs are presumably from admission (not stated). File 2: for the experimental dogs, the caption says "hpi" (hours post infection), but the number and timing of points are in the figure and were not read. Natural dogs in File 2 have no time axis stated. |
| Features | File 1, 17 columns: dog number (#), outcome, breed, age (years and months text, 7 months to 10 years 9 months), sex with neuter status (5 female, 10 male), body weight in kg (7 to 62.1), assumed location of infection from travel history (France 4, Hungary 2, Russia 1, Croatia 1, Serbia 1, no travel/Switzerland 6), then 0/1 clinical signs: apathy/lethargy (15/15), pale mucous membranes (15/15), pigmenturia (10/15), icterus (6/15), fever (5/15), anorexia (4/15), vomiting (4/15), water hammer pulse (4/15), epistaxis (3/15), plus body temperature in degrees C (37.8 to 40.5). | Data dictionary | Minimal. The caption gives the key "0: absent; 1: present", and units are shown for weight [kg], temperature [°C] and age (Y = years, Mo = months). Typos in the file: "Descritpion", "Fieber", "temeprature", "4 Y 8 Mio". Age is text, not a number. File 2 has a caption only; the IDs D1-3 and N1-11 are not linked to the # numbers in File 1. |
| Missingness | File 1 is nearly complete: 15 rows with no blank cells except neuter status, which is missing for dog #2 ("male") and dog #6 ("female"). Travel is recorded for all dogs. File 2 values cannot be assessed (figure only). Which dogs have WBC differentials is unclear: the caption names 11 natural dogs, but the article note uses 12 dogs (6 and 6) for haematology. |
| Technical requirements | Low skill but manual work. There is no spreadsheet. The PDF text comes out as one cell per line, and some cells wrap over two lines (breed, sex, location), so automatic parsing is error-prone. With only 15 rows, transcribing to CSV by hand and checking against the PDF page is the safest route. File 2 needs plot digitising (or the authors' data) to get any numbers. Images need a viewer; they were not opened. |
| Files | PMC4913656_supplementaryFiles.zip (local path data/raw/S-CB516/), 8 members, retrieved 2026-10-28: JVIM-30-174-s001.pdf (21,651 bytes, 1 page, 15-dog table), JVIM-30-174-s002.pdf (66,355 bytes, 1 page, caption plus figure panels), and 6 image files JVIM-30-174-g001, g002 and g003 as jpg and gif (7,353 to 89,316 bytes each; not opened). Sizes are the member sizes in the inspection summary. The zip's own size and SHA-256 are not in the files I was allowed to read. |
| Overlaps | The 15 natural dogs are the CB516 clinical cohort only (client-owned, Switzerland, 2011-2013 per the note). Cohort CH21 links CB516 with CB010 (proteomics of 3 experimentally inoculated Beagles, ENA P-PRJEB15861). The 3 experimental dogs D1-3 in File 2 may be the same animals as in CB010, but nothing in these files says so. The natural dogs are not part of that overlap. Two natural dogs are Beagles (#5, #11); they are client-owned and should not be confused with the experimental Beagles. The note says the experimental isolate came from a Bernese mountain dog that had travelled to Hungary; File 1 has one Bernese Mountain Dog (#15) with no travel history, so it is not that donor dog, and the donor is not identifiable in the file. Overlap with other Zurich clinical series is not established. |

**Downloaded files (1; 0.28 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB516/PMC4913656_supplementaryFiles.zip` (281,450 B, sha256 `d39a4bccc960…`)

**Suitable questions:** (1) Describe the clinical presentation of 15 Swiss B. canis cases by outcome (signs, temperature, age, weight, sex, breed, travel vs local infection), and check the article's statement that age, sex and clinical signs did not differ between survivors and nonsurvivors. (2) Use as a case-description table to accompany the article's laboratory results (but dogs cannot be linked to the article's per-group lab numbers). (3) Count local (no travel, 6) vs imported (9) cases and their countries. (4) If the plot is digitised, look at the shape of the WBC differential over hpi in 3 experimental dogs, and at survivors vs nonsurvivors; treat as rough. Not suitable: any prognostic model, biomarker work, mechanism, or the lactate/WBC claims (those values are not in the files), or merging with CB010 proteomics.

**Recommended methods:** Descriptive tables only. Transcribe the 15 rows by hand, double-check against the PDF, then report counts and medians by outcome. Fisher exact tests for yes/no signs and Mann-Whitney for age, weight and temperature are possible but have almost no power at 8 vs 7, so report them as descriptive with exact counts and do not adjust for anything. Convert age text to months. No machine learning, no clustering. For File 2, digitise the plot with a documented tool and state the error, or ask the authors for the underlying values.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Very small (15 dogs, 8 vs 7). No laboratory values, so the article's main findings (lactate, WBC, platelets, protein, phosphate, triglycerides) cannot be reproduced from these files. Clinical signs are yes/no only, with no severity grades, and apathy and pale mucous membranes are present in every dog, so they carry no information. Sampling time is not stated. "Died" mixes spontaneous death and euthanasia (per the article) and has no time to event. The dog IDs in File 2 (D1-3, N1-11) cannot be matched to File 1, and the 5 + 6 natural dogs in the File 2 caption disagree with the 6 + 6 in the note. File 2 is figure-only. Travel location is an "assumed" place of infection. Neuter status is missing for 2 dogs, and age is free text with typos. No treatment information in the files. Which dogs came from the Zurich clinic and which from private practices is not recorded. Selection: referral and private-practice cases of acute disease; most nonsurvivors died within 48 hours, so the outcome mostly reflects very severe disease at presentation.

**Unverified fields:** which of the 15 dogs were Zurich clinic cases (12) and which private-practice cases (3); whether 'Died' includes the 2 euthanised dogs (article: 6 spontaneous deaths, 2 euthanasias); fever cut-off (inferred between 38.6 and 39.1 degrees C from the 0/1 flag and temperature); time at which clinical signs were recorded (assumed admission); neuter status of dogs #2 and #6 (blank in the file); File 2 figure contents: cell types, values, number and timing of hpi points (text layer holds the caption only); File 2 caption counts (N1-5 survivors, N6-11 nonsurvivors = 11 dogs) vs 6 + 6 in the note; which dog is missing; mapping of D1-3 and N1-11 to the # numbers in File 1; whether experimental dogs D1-3 are the same Beagles as in CB010 (ENA P-PRJEB15861); content of the 6 image members (g001-g003, jpg and gif; identified by file name only); zip file size and SHA-256 (not in the files I was allowed to read); cell alignment of the File 1 dump (placed by header order; wrapped cells such as breed, sex, location were joined by judgement and numbers checked for plausibility); that 'no travel history (Switzerland)' means locally acquired infection (stated as an assumed location); dataset-specific licence (taken from the article open-access record); overlap of the 15 natural dogs with other Zurich clinical series

### S-CB013 — Shapiro et al. 2017 (BMC Vet Res 13:238) Additional file 4: per-dog qPCR Ct, PCV and platelet data, free-ranging dogs, NW NSW and Northern Territory, Australia

**Why ranked 19:** Per-dog PCV/platelets/qPCR in free-roaming Australian dogs (B. vogeli, A. platys); few positives.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | Public download verified |
| Linked articles | [CB013](articles/CB013_2017_vector-borne-zoonotic-diseases-north-west.md) |
| Correspondence check | Verified by recomputing from the xlsx. (1) 'All data' has 96 unique dog IDs; this equals the article's n=96, and the Ti Tree sheet has 51 IDs and Moree 45 IDs (disjoint, union identical to 'All data'), matching the article's NT n=51 and NSW n=45. (2) Babesia CT is present in exactly 7 dogs (2 Moree, 5 Ti Tree), matching '7 B. vogeli positive; 5 NT, 2 NSW'. (3) Anaplasma CT present in 26 dogs, matching A. platys n=26. (4) 4 of the 7 Babesia-positive dogs also have an Anaplasma CT (DOG 8, N27, N3, P12), matching 'four of 7 co-infected'. (5) Pearson r of Babesia CT vs PCV among the 7 positives is -0.871, matching the article's r = -0.87 (n=7 was unstated in the article). (6) Anaemia flags: 4/7 Babesia-positive vs 9/89 negative gives OR about 11.85, matching reported OR 11.8. (7) Babesia-positive mean PCV 0.316 vs 0.442 in negatives, consistent with the reported lower PCV. Discrepancy: the article note gives thrombocytopenia as <60 x10^9/L, but the PLT flag column marks 3 dogs with 63, 68 and 77 as 1 (18 flagged in total; lowest unflagged value 91), so the flag cut-off in the file is higher than the stated one (not documented in the file). Extra material: the Moree sheet (47 rows) and the Y51Y53 sheet hold two additional dogs (DOG Y51, DOG Y53) absent from 'All data', so the workbook has 98 distinct IDs; DOG Y51 is a further Babesia-positive dog (Ct 27.98). Their origin is not stated in the file. |

Anaemic (PCV flag) n=13; thrombocytopenic (PLT flag) n=18. There are no healthy-vs-disease clinical groups; dogs were generally healthy. |

| Technical requirements | Trivial: any spreadsheet or pandas/R read of the xlsx (24.6 kB). There is no sequence or omics annotation requirement. Binary and categorical codes need the article text for interpretation. Float artefacts (e.g. 0.45200000000000001) are cosmetic. |
| Files | PMC5558717_supplementaryFiles.zip (2,530,420 bytes; SHA-256 0353687ae78d4745341ae127eab79f5a1c03b8636b1a302fea18122121311e00; retrieved 2026-10-23). It contains 12 members. Data: 12917_2017_1169_MOESM4_ESM.xlsx (24,647 bytes) with sheets 'All data' (97x21), 'Moree' (48x9), 'Ti Tree' (52x9) and 'Y51Y53' (3x20). The rest is non-tabular: MOESM1-3 PNG photographs and Fig1-4 images (jpg/gif), which have no data content. |
| Overlaps | The Moree and Ti Tree sheets are subsets of 'All data' (same 96 dogs), with the two Y-dogs added to the Moree sheet. 'Moree' includes DOG Y51 and DOG Y53 although they are not in 'All data'; Y51Y53 is a duplicate for those two. There is a separate Bartonella-focused NT subset (n=40) in the article that is not identifiable in this file. The dogs may overlap with other CB-series datasets only if from the same cohort, which was not checked. |

**Downloaded files (1; 2.53 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB013/PMC5558717_supplementaryFiles.zip` (2,530,420 B, sha256 `0353687ae78d…`)

**Suitable questions:** Descriptive clinicopathology of B. vogeli and A. platys in naturally exposed free-ranging dogs: association of qPCR positivity with PCV and platelet count; co-infection patterns; effect of Ct (proxy for parasite load) on PCV (the reported r=-0.87 is from only 7 dogs and could be checked directly); site differences between NT and NSW; effect of body condition, tick burden, age and sex as covariates. Useful as a worked example of confounded field data and as a pooled estimate in an evidence synthesis, but not as a training set for prediction of clinical severity.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics are far more defensible than ML. Use exact/Fisher or Firth logistic regression for PCV<0.30 versus B. vogeli (4/7 vs 9/89), linear regression on continuous PCV and log platelet count with covariates for A. platys, site and body condition, Spearman rank correlation with bootstrap CI for Ct vs PCV (n=7), and a mixed model or site-stratified analysis. Haemoplasma and A. platys co-infection need to be modelled jointly. Penalised regression with nested CV is only sensible for exploratory effect-size estimation, and the sample size would not support stable prediction. Do not treat Ct as comparable between pathogens or runs.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n (96 dogs) and only 7 B. vogeli positives, with 4 co-infected with A. platys, so effects are inseparable and confidence intervals are very wide (small-n/low-p, not high-p: about 6 continuous variables). Confounding by site and sampling date (NT Nov 2010 vs NSW Feb 2013, with 5 of 7 positives in NT), undernutrition/body condition, ectoparasites, lactation and age (age is coded in four categories). Selection: convenience sample of free-ranging dogs; PCR-negative dogs were used for the thresholds, so the anaemia and thrombocytopenia flags are circular if used as labels. Outcome leakage: the PCV and PLT flags are deterministic functions of PCV and PLT, and the 'control', 'Polymicrobial', 'Myco status' and 'alone' columns are functions of the qPCR columns, so none should be used as both predictor and target. There is a single time point, no clinical outcome and no treatment data. Because the 'Babesia CT' column is blank for negatives, a Ct model is a positives-only model. There is no repeated-measures structure, but animal-level splitting still matters if N23A/N23B are the same dog. Validation needs an external cohort. Cross-species comparability is limited: the species is B. vogeli (a mild-to-subclinical agent) from Australian free-ranging dogs, mostly subclinical or co-infected, so it is not comparable to B. rossi or B. canis severe disease or to human or other-species babesiosis. The qPCR panel (commercial, target gene not stated) reports Ct, which is only a semi-quantitative proxy for parasitaemia.

**Unverified fields:** N23A/N23B identity (one dog or two); independent-animal count 95 vs 96; origin and inclusion status of DOG Y51 and DOG Y53 (appear in extra sheets only); meaning and direction of the 'PCR negative control dogs' column (inferred from rows); coding of Age, Breed, Gender, Entire/ster, BC and Ticks (no dictionary in file); units for PCV (L/L) and platelets (x10^9/L) (taken from article note, not the workbook); platelet flag cut-off (article note states <60; file flags values up to 77); sampling dates per site (taken from the article note, no date column); licence of the supplement itself (inferred from article CC BY); overlap with other CB datasets; whether the Bartonella NT n=40 subset is present (not identifiable); definition of 'Polymicrobial' beyond the column name

**Downloaded files (1; 0.26 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB106/PMC9889677_supplementaryFiles.zip` (258,551 B, sha256 `5566ec06a372…`)

**Suitable questions:** S-CB148: descriptive evidence extraction for the review, such as reference-interval-based abnormality prevalence at presentation (thrombocytopenia 100 percent, hypoalbuminaemia/anaemia, CRP elevation, hyperfibrinogenaemia, low iron), recovery kinetics of haematology, acute-phase, coagulation and antibody markers between T0, T1 and T2, and seroconversion timing; these can be used as benchmark effect sizes (median, IQR) for the mild/uncomplicated end of the severity spectrum, and for power calculations or priors. S-CB106: descriptive tabulation of post-imidocarb PCR persistence (7 of 9 positive after the first injection; persistence up to the 4th injection in one dog) for treatment-monitoring or PCR-clearance questions. Neither can address mechanism, subtyping, biomarker discovery or prediction of severity.

**Recommended methods:** Evidence synthesis only: tabulate proportions with exact (Clopper-Pearson) intervals from n/N, and use medians/IQRs as reported (approximate pooling by quantile estimation if combined with other studies, with caution as only medians, SD and IQR are provided). No ML is possible or defensible: S-CB148 has no animal-level rows (n=23, no outcome contrast), S-CB106 has 9 dogs and about 34 results. If row-level data were ever obtained from the authors, mixed-effects models with dog as random effect (or GEE) would be the appropriate analysis for T0/T1/T2, and exact or permutation tests for PCR persistence; penalised regression with nested CV only if a binary severity outcome with adequate events existed (it does not here).

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n and no animal-level data; S-CB148 reports only parameters with significant changes (selective reporting, so the table cannot be used to infer which analytes are normal); only medians/SDs, with skewed distributions (e.g. CK max 5214, reticulocytes) making SD uninformative; reference-interval categories are laboratory-specific (Laboklin) with no healthy controls; all dogs treated with imidocarb at varying dose, so T1/T2 improvement is confounded with treatment and natural resolution (no untreated comparator); T1/T2 timing varies (median 15 and 32 days); sample selection (laboratory-submitted samples, prospective enrolment of PCR-confirmed acute cases, uncomplicated course only), so spectrum bias and no severe cases; outcome leakage not applicable but any 'severity' analysis would be impossible since all were uncomplicated; if used with other data, animal-level (not sample-level) splitting would be needed for repeated measures; no external validation possible; co-infection excluded only 'as best as possible'. S-CB106 is a treatment-follow-up table in a referral population with irregular, clinician-driven PCR timing (informative sampling; dogs with persistent positivity were re-tested more often, which biases persistence estimates); the table includes only dogs with a positive follow-up PCR, so the denominator for persistence is the full cohort (49) from the article rather than the table. Cross-species comparability is untested; B. canis infection in dogs is distinct from human B. microti/B. divergens babesiosis, and any comparison is only conceptual (haemolysis, thrombocytopenia, acute-phase response). Licence CC BY-NC-ND for CB106 restricts derivatives. Do not describe any exploratory grouping from these data as subtypes.

**Unverified fields:** Exact number of laboratory parameters in S-CB148 table (row labels partly lost in text extraction; PDF not rendered visually because pdftoppm unavailable); n_samples=34 for S-CB106 (approximate count from text layout; Dog 9 block garbled); Meaning of 'Day of injection' and 'Day PCR after injection' reference origins in S-CB106 and of ND; Supplement-specific licence (article licence inferred to apply); Whether the 9 dogs of S-CB106 are exactly those with positive follow-up PCR (inferred from table title); Possible overlap of dogs between CB106 and earlier outbreak publication or other included datasets; Content of gif/jpg figure members (not examined as data); Whether S-CB148 T1/T2 values for non-significant parameters exist elsewhere (article full text not re-read)

_Assessed jointly with the other PDF supplement in one agent run; fields may mix both — see correspondence_evidence._

**Downloaded files (1; 0.63 MB):** `data/raw/S-CB005/PMC5379697_supplementaryFiles.zip` (627,600 B, sha256 `11ac2a04abd3…`)

**Suitable questions:** Descriptive epidemiology only. Examples: PCR prevalence (B. canis 7/109, Theileria 15/109) with exact (Clopper-Pearson) CIs; associations of positivity with region, age or sex; B. canis versus Theileria distribution by habitat; cause-of-death pattern in positives and negatives. It is a useful reference for wild-canid host tolerance, but it cannot support questions on pathophysiology, severity, biomarkers or prognosis. The single clinically severe case (wolf 109) is a case report, not a group.

**Recommended methods:** Ordinary statistics only: exact binomial CIs, Fisher or exact trend tests, logistic or Firth regression for age, sex and region with very few B. canis events (7), and multiple imputation or complete-case analysis noted for '?' ages. ML is not defensible: 109 animals, 7 B. canis events, 6 weak categorical variables, and no continuous phenotype.

**Limitations (small n/high p, batch, confounding, leakage, splitting, validation, cross-species):** Small n and only 7 B. canis events (6 excluding the zoo wolf), giving wide CIs. Selection and survivorship bias: carcasses are mostly roadkill and hunted wolves, so the sample is not representative and cannot separate subclinical carriage from disease. Confounding: age, captivity and region are entangled with the only severe case. Outcome leakage: 'babesiosis' cause of death is the same case as the zoo positive, and 'live animal' appears in the cause-of-death field. No clinical, haematological, histological or parasitaemia data. No collection date, so temporal and batch effects cannot be assessed. Wolf age is estimated and missing for 14. Regional sheets duplicate the master sheet. No external validation is possible; animal-level splitting is trivial (one row per animal) but pointless at this size. Cross-species comparability to dogs is poor, since wolf data are post-mortem and lack haematology, there is no case definition, and T. capreoli-like infections are not canine babesiosis. Piroplasm species were confirmed by sequencing in the article, but the sequences are not in this file.

**Unverified fields:** Full wolf-finding place strings (truncated at 25 characters in dump output); Collection years per wolf (not in file; article states 1996-2015); Whether the regional sheet region assignments match the article's regional definitions exactly; Licence of the supplement itself (the article licence is CC BY, applied by assumption); Figure image contents (not inspected)

## Remaining articles: negative and unassessed checks

Status 4 = full text, publisher page, supplement list or repository links were checked and no dataset was found (statements such as 'all data are in the article' count as no separate dataset). Status 5 = full text not accessible (paywall, bot protection or no online full text), so only the abstract was assessed. A few status-4 articles print per-dog values in article tables (small case series); these are noted in the article files but not catalogued as datasets.
